estimate (or Morawetz estimate) for good scalar fields using the standard
multipliers
T
and
f(r)Z
.

Proposition 4.4

(Energy boundedness and integrated local energy decay for good scalar fields)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
satisfies the assumptions of

definition

4.1
with inhomogeneity
\tilde{F}
. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
, we have

(4.5)

\begin{split}&\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{2},v)+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\delta{}}((L\Phi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2})+r^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}(\Phi^{2}+(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta{}}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},0}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v).\end{split}

Moreover, we have

(4.6)

\begin{split}&\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\delta{}}(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta}{}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\delta{}}(L\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}\lvert T\Phi{}\rvert\lvert\tilde{F}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Remark 4.5

(Higher-order estimates)
.

We only prove the estimates in
eq.

4.6
for the good scalar field

\Phi{}
itself, and do not prove higher-order analogues that include
commutation. Instead, we prove higher-order estimates for the
r^{p}
-weighted
energy in
section

4.1.3
. In most situations, we will use the
p=0
energy

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\Phi{}]
as a replacement for the
T
-energy

\tilde{E}[\Phi{}]
.

Remark 4.6

(Control of terms involving
\underline{L}\Phi{}
)
.

The importance of the estimates in
eq.

4.6
is their control of
flux terms on
\Sigma{}(\tau{})
as well as bulk terms that involve

\underline{L}\Phi{}
, since these quantities are not controlled by the

r^{p}
-weighted energy.

Proof.

Step 1: Energy boundedness.
In this step we will prove
eq.

4.6
and
also the statement that for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}

and
v\geq 0
, we have

(4.7)

\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{2},v)+\int_{\underline{C}(v)\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}\Phi^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta{}}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{1},v)+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}\lvert T\Phi{}\rvert\lvert\tilde{F}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

In Step 2, we will use
eq.

4.7
to conclude

eq.

4.5
.

Let
f:(0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}
be a bounded non-increasing
C^{1}
function.
Multiply
eq.

4.2
by
4f(r)T\Phi{}
, and then use the Leibniz rule and

lemma

2.2
to get

(4.8)

\begin{split}4fT\Phi{}\tilde{F}&=T\bigl(f(r)[(2-Gh)(L\Phi{})^{2}+Gh(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}+2\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi^{2}+r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}\Phi{})^{2}]\bigr)\\
&\qquad+GX\bigl(f(r)[(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}-(L\Phi{})^{2}]\bigr)+\partial{}_{\theta}{}(\cdots{})+(-f^{\prime})[(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}-(L\Phi{})^{2}]\end{split}

Let
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
and integrate
eq.

4.8
over the
region
\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
with respect to the volume form
G^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
.
The boundary term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
vanishes as
r_{0}\to 0
by the boundary
conditions in
eq.

4.1
. By
eq.

4.4
, the boundary
term at
\{v=v_{0}\}
, with integrand
(L\Phi{})^{2}+\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi^{2}+r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta{}}\Phi{})^{2}
, has a good sign since
f(r)>0
, and it is comparable to the one in
eq.

4.7
when

f(r)\equiv 1
. The boundary terms at
\{\tau{}=\tau_{i}\}
(
i=1,2
)
are comparable to
\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{i},v)
when
f(r)\equiv 1
by

eq.

4.4
. Thus we obtain
eq.

4.7
by taking
f(r)\equiv 1
. To obtain
eq.

4.6
, take
f(r)=1+(1+r)^{-\delta{}}
.

Step 2: Integrated local energy decay.
Let
f:(0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}
be a bounded
non-decreasing
C^{1}
function. Multiply
eq.

4.2
by

2f(r)(L-\underline{L})\Phi{}
, and then use the Leibniz rule to get

(4.9)

\begin{split}2f(r)F(L-\underline{L})\Phi{}&=\underline{L}(f(L\Phi{})^{2}-\alpha{}fr^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi^{2}-fr^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2})\\
&\qquad+L(-f(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}+\alpha{}fr^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi^{2}+fr^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2})\\
&\qquad-2G\alpha{}(fr^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r)))^{\prime}\Phi^{2}-2G(fr^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r)))^{\prime}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+Gf^{\prime}[(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}+(L\Phi{})^{2}]+\partial{}_{\theta}{}(\cdots{})\end{split}

Let
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
and integrate
eq.

4.9
over the
region
\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
with respect to the volume form
G^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
.
The boundary term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
vanishes as
r_{0}\to 0
by the boundary
conditions in
eq.

4.1
. The boundary terms at

\{\tau{}=\tau_{i}\}
and
\{v=v_{0}\}
are controlled by the left-hand side of

eq.

4.7
. We now consider the bulk terms. Choose
f=1-(1+r)^{-\delta{}}
, so that
f^{\prime}\gtrsim_{\delta}{}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\delta{}}

and
-(fr^{-2})^{\prime}\gtrsim r^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}
. Since
f_{i}(r)=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{})
, we
conclude using
eq.

4.4
that

(4.10)

\begin{split}&\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau_{2},v)+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\delta{}}((L\Phi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2})+r^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}(\Phi^{2}+(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(\lvert L\Phi{}\rvert+\lvert\underline{L}\Phi{}\rvert)\lvert F\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Using Young’s inequality and noting that

(4.11)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{1+\delta{}}\lvert F\rvert^{2}\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},0}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)

completes the proof of
eq.

4.5
.
∎

4.1.3.
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates

In this section, we prove
r^{p}
-weighted estimates for good scalar fields.
Since these quantities satisfy equations with a zeroth-order term of a
favourable sign, these estimates follow from standard techniques.

We first derive the equation satisfied by the commuted quantities
(rL)^{N}\Phi{}
.

Lemma 4.7

(Equation satisfied by a good scalar field after commutation with
(rL)
)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}
solves
eq.

4.2
for some
\alpha{}\in\mathbf{R}
and

f_{1},f_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}\langle{}r\rangle^{-a})
. Then for
N\geq 0
, we have

(4.12)

\begin{split}0&=\underline{L}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}+GNr^{-1}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}+\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))(rL)^{N}\Phi{}-r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}(rL)^{n}\tilde{F},\end{split}

for some constants
C_{N,n}\in\mathbf{R}
, where the implicit constants in the

\mathcal{O}
-notation depend on
\alpha{}
,
N
, and the functions
f_{1}
and

f_{2}
.

Proof.

We induct on
N
. The
N=0
case is immediate from
eq.

4.2
. Now
suppose
eq.

4.12
holds for some
N\geq 0
. Multiplying both
sides of the equation by
r
and using the Leibniz rule for the first term
gives

(4.13)

\begin{split}0&=\underline{L}(rL)^{N+1}\Phi{}+G(N+1)L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}+\alpha{}r^{-1}(1+f_{1}(r))(rL)^{N}\Phi{}-r^{-1}(1+f_{2}(r))\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}r(rL)^{n}\tilde{F},\end{split}

Act with
L
on both sides and rewrite

(4.14)

LL(rL)^{N}\Phi{}=r^{-1}L(rL)^{N+1}\Phi{}-r^{-1}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}

to obtain
eq.

4.12
with
N+1
in place of
N
. In
particular, we use the fact that
f_{i}(r)=\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle^{-a})
for
i=1,2
to obtain
L(r^{-1}(1+f_{i}(r)))=\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})
.
∎

Proposition 4.8

(
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
satisfies the assumptions of
definition

4.1
with inhomogeneity
\tilde{F}
. Let
p\in[0,2-\eta_{0}]
(where the small constant
\eta_{0}>0
was fixed in

section

2.1
). Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,
v\geq 0
, and
N\geq 0
, we have

(4.15)

\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(n_{2}+p)\langle{}r\rangle^{p}r^{-1}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{p}r^{-3}(\partial{}_{\theta{}}\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}\Phi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{p}r^{-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\alpha{},\eta{}_{0},N}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v),\end{split}

where the norm
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}_{p,N}
was defined in

section

2.7.3
. It follows that for
p\in(0,2-\eta{}_{0}]
, we have

(4.16)

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p-1,N}[\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\alpha{},p,\eta{}_{0},N}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v).

Proof.

Step 1: Preliminary reductions.
First, note that
eq.

4.16

follows immediately from
eq.

4.15
, since
h(r)
is bounded. To
establish
eq.

4.15
, it suffices to consider the case where

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}](\tau_{2},v)
is replaced by

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}[(rL)^{N}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)
and
n_{1}=0
and
n_{2}=N
in the sum on the left-hand side. This is because
\partial_{\theta}{}\Phi{}

satisfies the assumptions of
definition

4.1
whenever
\Phi{}
does (in
particular since
\partial_{\theta}{}
commutes with equation
eq.

4.2
).

Moreover, it suffices to prove the estimates with
\langle{}r\rangle^{p}
in
the bulk term on the left-hand side of
eq.

4.15
replaced with
r^{p}

and with
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}

replaced with
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\circ}
,
where

(4.17)

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\coloneqq{}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}(L\Phi{})^{2}+h(r)r^{p-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}+h(r)r^{p-2}\Phi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}

and the higher-order version
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}^{\circ}
is defined as in

section

2.7.2
. This is because the estimate for
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}
can
obtained by summing the estimates for
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}^{\circ}
and

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{{p,N}}^{\circ}
.

Step 2: Zeroth-order estimate.
We first establish
eq.

4.15
(with
the appropriate reductions of Step 1) when
N=0
.
Multiply
eq.

4.2
by
2r^{p}L\Phi{}
and use the Leibniz rule to get

(4.18)

\begin{split}r^{p}L\Phi{}\cdot\tilde{F}&=\underline{L}(r^{p}(L\Phi{})^{2})+L(r^{p-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}+\alpha{}r^{p-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi{}^{2})+\partial{}_{\theta}{}(\cdots{})+Gpr^{p-1}(L\Phi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+G(2-p)r^{p-3}[\alpha{}(1+f_{1}(r)-(2-p)^{-1}rf_{1}^{\prime}(r))\Phi{}^{2}+(1+f_{2}(r)-(2-p)^{-1}rf_{2}^{\prime}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}].\end{split}

Fix
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
. We integrate
eq.

4.18
over the region

\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
with
respect to
G^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
. The boundary term on

\{\tau{}=\tau_{i}\}
is proportional to

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{i},v)
by
eq.

4.4
. The bulk
term that arises is proportional to the bulk term in
eq.

4.15

(with the appropriate reductions of Step 1) when

N=0
by
eq.

4.4
, after one chooses
\epsilon{}\ll 2-p\leq\eta{}_{0}
, so that

(4.19)

1+f_{1}(r)-(2-p)^{-1}rf_{1}^{\prime}(r)=1+O(\epsilon{}\max(1,(2-p)^{-1}))\geq\frac{1}{2}.

The boundary
term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
is
r^{p}[(L\Phi{})^{2}-r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}-\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi{}^{2}]
. By the boundary conditions
eq.

4.1
, this term vanishes as

r_{0}\to 0
. Applying
eq.

4.4
to the boundary term at

\{v=v_{0}\}
, we have

(4.20)

\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}pr^{p-1}(L\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-3}\Phi{}^{2}+r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\underline{C}(v)\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}r^{p-2}\Phi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim_{\alpha{}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\Big\lvert\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}L\Phi{}\cdot\tilde{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Big\rvert.\end{split}

To control the bulk term involving the inhomogeneity
F
, we can use Young’s
inequality directly when
p\geq\eta{}_{0}
:

(4.21)

\Big\lvert\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}L\Phi{}\cdot\tilde{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Big\rvert\leq\delta{}p\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p-1}(L\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\delta{}^{-1}\eta{}_{0}^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p+1}\tilde{F}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

The first term on the right can be absorbed into the left-hand side of

eq.

4.20
(for
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small depending on

\alpha{}
), and the second term on the right is controlled by the norm

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,0}[F](\tau_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)
. If
p\leq\eta_{0}
, then we first
integrate the
L
-derivative by parts and then use Young’s inequality:

(4.22)

\begin{split}&\Big\lvert\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}L\Phi{}\cdot\tilde{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Big\rvert\\
&\lesssim\delta{}\Bigl[\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p-3}\Phi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{2},v)}h(r)r^{p-2}\Phi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\underline{C}(v_{0})\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}r^{p-2}\Phi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr]\\
&\qquad+\delta{}^{-1}\Bigl[\sum_{n=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p+1}((rL)^{n}F)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{i},v)}h(r)r^{p+2}\tilde{F}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{1},v)}h(r)r^{p-2}\Phi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\underline{C}(v)\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}r^{p+2}\tilde{F}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr].\end{split}

The terms with
\delta{}
can be absorbed into the left-hand side of

eq.

4.20
, and the terms with
\delta^{-1}
make up the norm

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,0}[F](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)
(after noting that
h(r)\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}
). In either case, we obtain the
N=0
case of

eq.

4.15
.

Step 3: Higher-order estimates.
Now let
N\geq 1
and suppose

eq.

4.15
(with the appropriate reductions of Step 1) holds for

N-1
in place of
N
. Multiply
eq.

4.12
by

2r^{p}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}
and use the Leibniz rule to get

(4.23)

\begin{split}0&=\underline{L}(r^{p}(L(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2})+L\bigl(\alpha{}r^{p-2}(1+f_{1}(r))((rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-2}(1+f_{2}(r))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}\bigr)+\partial{}_{\theta}{}(\cdots{})\\
&\qquad+2G(N+p)r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}+G\alpha{}(2-p)r^{p-3}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}\max(1,(2-p)^{-1})))((rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+G(2-p)r^{p-3}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}\max(1,(2-p)^{-1})))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+r^{(p-1)/2}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\cdot\Bigl[\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{(p-3)/2})(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{(p-3)/2})(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{n}\Phi{})\Bigr]\\
&\qquad+r^{p}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\cdot\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}(rL)^{n}\tilde{F},\end{split}

Fix
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
and integrate over
\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
. As in Step 2, the boundary term at

\{r=r_{0}\}
vanishes as
r_{0}\to 0
. After using

eq.

4.4
to show that the bulk term and the boundary terms at

\{\tau{}=\tau_{i}\}
and
\{v=v_{0}\}
are coercive, we get

(4.24)

\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[(rL)^{N}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-3}((rL)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\underline{C}(v_{0})\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}r^{p-2}((rL)^{N}\Phi)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p}^{\circ}[(rL)^{N}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{(p-1)/2}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\cdot\Bigl[\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{(p-3)/2})(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{(p-3)/2})(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{n}\Phi{})\Bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\cdot C_{N,n}{(rL)}^{n}\tilde{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

After using Young’s inequality and adding a suitable multiple of

eq.

4.15
(with the appropriate reductions of Step 1) with
N-1

in place of
N
(applied to both
u
and
\partial_{\theta}{}u
) to control
the terms on the second line of the right-hand side of
eq.

4.24
, we
obtain

(4.25)

\begin{split}\textup{{(LHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{rp-prelim-inequality}}\text{{)}}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{1}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N-1}^{\circ}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{n}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}r^{p}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}\cdot{(rL)}^{n}\tilde{F}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Controlling the bulk term on the right by integrating by parts as in Step 2
completes the proof of
eq.

4.15
, in view of Step 1.
∎

4.1.4.
An integrated estimate for the modified
T
-energy

The energy estimates of
sections

4.1.2
and
4.1.3
allow us to quickly
prove an estimate controlling the integral of the modified

T
-energy.

Proposition 4.9

(Integrated estimate for the modified
T
-energy)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
satisfies the assumptions of

definition

4.1
with inhomogeneity
\tilde{F}
. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
, we have

(4.26)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\delta}{}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}[\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},0}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v).

Proof.

Since
h(r)\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\eta{}_{h}}
, we have

(4.27)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(L\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}\Phi^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\eta{}_{h}}(\underline{L}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

To control the final term on the right-hand side, use
eq.

4.6

(with
\eta_{h}
in place of
\delta{}
there) to obtain

(4.28)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{E}[\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(L\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}\Phi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert T\Phi{}\rvert\lvert\tilde{F}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

To control the first three terms on the right-hand side, use
eq.

4.16
with
p=1
. For the final term, use an
r
-weighted Young’s inequality together
with
eq.

4.5
.
∎

4.2.
Energy estimates for radially symmetric scalar fields

We now turn our attention to radially
symmetric scalar fields, for which proving energy estimates is more difficult,
as discussed in
section

1.2
.

4.2.1.
Energy boundedness and integrated energy estimates

We first record the standard
T
-energy estimate. For a higher-order version
of this estimate (controlling the energy
E_{N}[\varphi{}]
for
N\geq 1
), see

proposition

4.13
.

Proposition 4.10

(Boundedness of the
T
-energy)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. For
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and
v\geq 0
, we have

(4.29)

E[\varphi{}](\tau_{2},v)+\sup_{0\leq v^{\prime}\leq v}\int_{\underline{C}(v^{\prime})\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}}r(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}+r^{-1}(\partial_{\theta}{}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim E[\varphi{}](\tau_{1},v)+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert\lvert r\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

Moreover, for any
\delta{}>0
we have

(4.30)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1-\delta{}}r(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta}{}E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1-\delta{}}r(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-\delta{}}\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert\lvert r\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let
f:(0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}
be a bounded non-increasing
C^{1}
function. Multiply

eq.

2.20
by
-4rf(r)T\varphi{}
, and then use the Leibniz rule and

lemma

2.2
to get

(4.31)

\begin{split}-4rfT\varphi{}\Box{}\varphi{}&=\underline{L}(fr(L\varphi{})^{2}+fr^{-1}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{})^{2})+L(fr(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}+fr^{-1}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{})^{2})\\
&\qquad-\partial{}_{\theta}{}(2fr^{-2}\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{}T\varphi{})+G(-f^{\prime})r[(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}-(L\varphi{})^{2}]\\
&=T(fr(2-h)(L\varphi{})^{2}+frh(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}+fr^{-1}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{})^{2})+GX(-fr(L\varphi{})^{2}+fr(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2})\\
&\qquad+\partial{}_{\theta}{}(\cdots{})+G(-f^{\prime})r[(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}-(L\varphi{})^{2}].\end{split}

Let
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
and integrate
eq.

4.31

over the region
\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
with respect to the volume form
G^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
. The boundary term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
is
-fr(L\varphi{})^{2}+fr(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}
, which vanishes as
r_{0}\to 0
due to the
regularity of
\varphi{}
at
\{r=0\}
. To obtain

eq.

4.29
, take
f(r)\equiv 1
and take a supremum over

v_{0}\leq v
. To obtain
eq.

4.30
, take
f(r)=(1+r)^{-\delta{}}
.
∎

We now establish an estimate that controls a bulk term involving derivatives of

\varphi{}
by the
T
-energy of
\varphi{}
and a bulk term involving

\Psi{}
. We need this estimate to control terms arising from commutation (see
proposition

4.13
).

Proposition 4.11

(Integrated energy estimate; radially symmetric case)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be radially symmetric. For
0\leq\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}_{2}
,
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}\in(0,1)
, we have

(4.32)

\begin{split}&E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}[(L\varphi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta}{}E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\Psi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\lvert r\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{},\end{split}

Proof.

Take
f(r)=(1+r)^{-1+\delta{}}
, so that
f
is bounded and
(rf)^{\prime}\sim_{\delta}{}(1+r)^{-1+\delta{}}
. Rewrite
eq.

2.20
as

(4.33)

\underline{L}L\varphi{}=r^{-1}GZ\varphi{}-\Box{}\varphi{}=r^{-3/2}\Psi{}-\Box{}\varphi{}.

We have reinterpreted the first-order term
r^{-1}GZ\varphi{}
, which obstructs a
standard Morawetz estimate, as a source term involving
\Psi{}
, which we have
already controlled. Multiply
eq.

4.33
by
2rf(r)(L-\underline{L})\varphi{}=4f(r)r^{1/2}\Psi{}
and use the Leibniz rule and
Young’s inequality to get

(4.34)

\begin{split}\underline{L}(rf(L\varphi{})^{2})-L(rf(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2})+G(rf)^{\prime}[(L\varphi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}]&=4r^{-1}f\Psi^{2}+4f\cdot r^{-1/2}\Psi{}\cdot r\Box{}\varphi{}\\
&\lesssim r^{-1}f\Psi^{2}+f\lvert r\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}.\end{split}

Fix
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
. The boundary term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
arising from
integrating
eq.

4.34
over the region
\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
is
rf(L\varphi{})^{2}-rf(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}
, which vanishes as
r_{0}\to 0
. The boundary
terms at
\{\tau{}=\tau{}_{i}\}
are controlled by the
T
-energy, since

f
and
G
are bounded. After adding a suitable multiple of

eq.

4.29
, we obtain
eq.

4.32
.
∎

Next, we turn to proving a higher-order version of

eqs.

4.30
and
4.32
. To this end, we compute the terms
that arise when commuting the above estimates with
(rL)^{N}
.

Lemma 4.12

(Commutation with
(rL)^{N}
)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. Then for
N\geq 0
, we have

(4.35)

r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(1)(rL)^{n}\Psi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{1/2})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}

and

(4.36)

L(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{})=\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(1)L(rL)^{n}\Psi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}.

Moreover, if
\varphi{}
is radially symmetric, we have

(4.37)

[\Box{},(rL)^{N}]\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\bigl[\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})L(rL)^{n}\Psi{}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-2})(rL)^{n}\Psi{}\bigr].

We interpret sums from
0
to
N-1
as being empty if
N=0
.

Proof.

First,
eq.

4.35
follows from the stronger statement that for each
N\geq 1

and
0\leq n\leq N
, there exist constants
C_{N,n}
such that

(4.38)

r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}(rL)^{n}\Psi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{1/2})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}.

The
N=1
case of
eq.

4.38
follows from the computation

(4.39)

r^{1/2}GZ(rL)\varphi{}=(rL)\Psi{}-\frac{1}{2}\Psi{}+r^{1/2}GL\varphi{},

and the general case follows by induction on
N
after using

eq.

4.39
with
(rL)^{N}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
to write

(4.40)

\begin{split}r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N+1}\varphi{}&=r^{1/2}GZ(rL)(rL)^{N}\varphi{}=(rL)(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{})-\frac{1}{2}r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{}+r^{1/2}GL(rL)^{N}\varphi{}.\end{split}

Now
eq.

4.36
is obtained by acting with
L
on both sides of
eq.

4.35
.

The
N=1
case of
eq.

4.37
follows from the computation

(4.41)

[\Box{},rL]\varphi{}=(G^{2}+GrG^{\prime})r^{-1}L\varphi{}+2r^{-1/2}GL\Psi{}-r^{-1/2}GG^{\prime}\Psi{},

which can be obtained from
eq.

2.20
, and the inequality

a\geq 1
(where
a
appears in
section

2.2
). The general case
follows by induction on
N
after writing

(4.42)

[\Box{},(rL)^{N+1}\varphi{}]=(rL)[\Box{},(rL)^{N}]\varphi{}+[\Box{},rL](rL)^{N}\varphi{}

and using the identities
eqs.

4.36
and
4.35
to compute the final term in

eq.

4.42
.
∎

Proposition 4.13

(Higher-order
T
-energy estimate and integrated energy estimate; radially symmetric case)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,

v\geq 0
,
N\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
, we have

(4.43)

\begin{split}&E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}[(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)+\mathcal{A}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v),\end{split}

where
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZ\Box{}\varphi{}
is the inhomogeneity in the equation
satisfied by
\Psi{}
(see
eq.

2.26
) and the inhomogeneous norms

\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
were defined in

section

2.7.3
.

Proof.

First, the
N=0
case follows from
eqs.

4.32
and
4.16
. Now
suppose that
N\geq 1
and that we have established
eq.

4.43

with
N-1
in place of
N
. Apply
eq.

4.32
with
(rL)^{N}\varphi{}

in place of
\varphi{}
to obtain

(4.44)

\begin{split}&E[(rL)^{N}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}[(L(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta}{}E[(rL)^{N}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\lvert r[\Box{},(rL)^{N}]\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}r^{2}\lvert(rL)^{N}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

We now control the bulk terms on the right-hand side of
eq.

4.44
. Use

eq.

4.35
and
eq.

4.16
applied to
\Psi{}
(with
p=1+\delta{}
) to compute

(4.45)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}((rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Next, use
eq.

4.37
and
eq.

4.16
applied to
\Psi{}
(with
p=1+\delta{}
) to compute

(4.46)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\lvert r[\Box{},(rL)^{N}]\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}r(L(rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-4+\delta{}}((rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

To complete the proof of
eq.

4.43
, substitute

eqs.

4.45
and
4.46
into
eq.

4.44
and
add a suitable multiple of
eq.

4.43
with
N-1
in place of

N
.
∎

4.2.2.
r
-weighted energy estimates

We now establish
r
-weighted energy estimates for
radially symmetric scalar fields. We establish these estimates not for

\varphi{}
itself, but for
(rL)\varphi{}
(see
proposition

4.15
) and

T\varphi{}
(see
proposition

4.16
). That is, we must commute with one
derivative in order to obtain an
r
-weighted energy estimate.

We begin by deriving a general identity associated to multipliers of the form

f(r)L
.

Lemma 4.14

(Identity associated to the multiplier
f(r)L
)
.

Let
f(r)=rg(r)
for
g:[0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}
a
C^{2}
function. Then for any

v\geq 0
and
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, an identity of the following form
holds for radially symmetric functions
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
:

(4.47)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{2},v)}(2-Gh)f(L\psi{})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}G^{2}h(2rg^{\prime}+(1+O(\epsilon{}))g)\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}rf^{\prime}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\underline{C}(v)\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}\frac{1}{4}G^{2}(2rg^{\prime}+(1+O(\epsilon{}))g)\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&=\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{1},v)}(2-Gh)f(L\psi{})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}G^{2}h(2rg^{\prime}+(1+O(\epsilon{}))g)\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\bigl(O(1)rg^{\prime\prime}+O(1)g^{\prime}+O(\epsilon{}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-a})g\bigr)\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad-\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}2G^{-1}fL\psi{}r^{1/2}\Box{}\varphi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Proof.

Multiply both sides of
eq.

2.22
by
-2f(r)L\psi{}
, use the expansions

(4.48)

L\psi{}=\frac{1}{2}Gr^{-1/2}\varphi{}+r^{1/2}L\varphi{},\qquad(L\psi{})^{2}=r(L\varphi{})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}GL(\varphi{}^{2})+\frac{1}{4}G^{2}r^{-1}\varphi^{2},

and use the Leibniz rule to obtain

(4.49)

\begin{split}-2fL\psi{}r^{1/2}\Box{}\varphi{}&=\underline{L}(f(L\psi{})^{2})+\frac{1}{4}L(-r^{-1}fG(G-2rG^{\prime})\varphi^{2})+Grf^{\prime}(L\varphi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{4}G\Bigl[r(r^{-2}fG(G-2rG^{\prime}))^{\prime}+G^{2}r^{-1}f^{\prime}\Bigr]\varphi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}G^{2}f^{\prime}L(\varphi^{2})\\
&=\underline{L}(f(L\psi{})^{2})+\frac{1}{4}L\bigl(G^{2}(2rg^{\prime}+g+2rgG^{-1}G^{\prime})\varphi^{2}\bigr)+Grf^{\prime}(L\varphi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2}G^{3}\Bigl[-rg^{\prime\prime}-(1+3rG^{-1}G^{\prime})g^{\prime}-gG^{-1}(rG^{\prime\prime}+rG^{-1}(G^{\prime})^{2}+G^{\prime})\Bigr]\varphi{}^{2}\end{split}

In passing to the final line, we expressed
f=rg
and differentiated the

L(\varphi^{2})
term by parts, which produces a term
\frac{1}{2}(G^{2}f^{\prime})^{\prime}\varphi^{2}

and a total-
L
-derivative term. By the assumptions on
G
(and

lemma

2.3
), the zeroth-order term (in the last line of

eq.

4.49
) has the form

(4.50)

\bigl(O(1)rg^{\prime\prime}+O(1)g^{\prime}+O(\epsilon{}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-a})g\bigr)\varphi^{2}.

Now let
r_{0}>0
and
v_{0}>0
. Use
eq.

4.48
and the identity
f=rg
to express the integrand of the boundary term at
\{r=r_{0}\}
that
arises from integrating
eq.

4.49
on
\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}\cap\{v\leq v_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{0}\}
with respect to the volume
form
G^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
as follows:

(4.51)

f(L\psi{})^{2}-\frac{1}{4}G^{2}(2rg^{\prime}+g+2rgG^{-1}G^{\prime})\varphi^{2}=r^{2}g(L\varphi{})^{2}+Gf\varphi{}L\varphi{}-\frac{1}{4}G^{2}(2rg^{\prime}+2rgG^{-1}G^{\prime})\varphi^{2}.

This term vanishes as
r\to 0
since
g
and
G
are
C^{1}
functions of

r
(up to and including
r=0
). We therefore obtain the desired identity
upon integration.
∎

We now prove an estimate for
(rL)
-derivatives of the solution. We can close
the estimate because with
(rL)
will produce bulk terms involving
L(rL)^{\leq 1}\varphi{}
of a good sign as well as error terms involving
\Psi{}
.

Proposition 4.15

(
r
-weighted energy estimate for
(rL)
-derivatives; radially symmetric case)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,

v\geq 0
, and
N\geq 0
, and
\delta{}\geq 0
sufficiently small, we have

(4.52)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{N}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)+\mathcal{A}_{3+\delta{},N+1}[\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{},\end{split}

where
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZ\Box{}\varphi{}
is the inhomogeneity in the equation
satisfied by
\Psi{}
(see
eq.

2.26
) and the inhomogeneous norms

\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
were defined in

section

2.7.3
.

Proof.

Step 1: The case
N=0
.
Set
f(r)=r(1+r)^{\delta}{}
(i.e.
g(r)=(1+r)^{\delta}{}
).
We explicitly compute

(4.53)

\begin{cases}2rg^{\prime}(r)+(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))g(r)\sim\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{},\\
rf^{\prime}(r)\geq r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\\
\lvert rg^{\prime\prime}\rvert+\lvert g^{\prime}\rvert+\epsilon{}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-a}\lvert g\rvert\lesssim(\delta{}+\epsilon{})\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}.\end{cases}

It follows from
eq.

4.47
(after dropping the term on

\underline{C}(v)
, which has a good sign) that

(4.54)

\begin{split}&C^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+C^{-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\leq C\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+C(\delta{}+\epsilon{})\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}-\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}2G^{-1}r(1+r)^{\delta}{}L\psi{}r^{1/2}\Box{}\varphi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\end{split}

for some constant
C>0
. Applying
eq.

4.54
to
(rL)\varphi{}
in
place of
\varphi{}
, we obtain

(4.55)

\begin{split}&C^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+C^{-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(L(rL)\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\leq C\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+C(\delta{}+\epsilon{})\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad-\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}2G^{-1}r(1+r)^{\delta}{}L(r^{1/2}(rL)\varphi{})r^{1/2}\Box{}(rL)\varphi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\end{split}

We now compute the final term on the right-hand side of
eq.

4.55

and observe that it produces a bulk term (of a good sign) that controls

\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}((rL)\varphi{})^{2}
. The commutation formula
eq.

4.41
and the
identity

(4.56)

L(r^{1/2}(rL)\varphi{})=r^{1/2}L(rL)\varphi{}+\frac{1}{2}r^{1/2}L\varphi{}

imply

(4.57)

\begin{split}&G^{-1}r(1+r)^{\delta}{}L(r^{1/2}(rL)\varphi{})r^{1/2}[\Box{},rL]\varphi{}\\
&=(1+r)^{\delta}{}\Bigl(r^{1/2}L(rL)\varphi{}+\frac{1}{2}r^{1/2}L\varphi{}\Bigr)((G+rG^{\prime})r^{1/2}L\varphi{}+2rL\Psi{}-rG^{\prime}\Psi{})\\
\end{split}

We now expand the parentheses, producing terms proportional to

(4.58)

\textnormal{(I)}\coloneqq{}r(1+r)^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\textnormal{ and }\textnormal{(II)}\coloneqq{}(1+r)^{\delta}{}\cdot r^{1/2}L(rL)\varphi{}\cdot r^{1/2}L\varphi{}=\frac{1}{2}(1+r)^{\delta}{}L(((rL)\varphi{})^{2}),

with proportionality constants independent of
\delta{}
. We also produce terms
multiplying
L^{\leq 1}\Psi{}
, which we treat as error. The key step is to
differentiate by parts term
(II)
,
producing a total-
L
-derivative term of a good sign and a bulk term of a bad
sign proportional to
\delta{}r(1+r)^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}
, which is
compensated for by the pre-existing such term of a good sign that comes without
a
\delta{}
-factor, namely term
(I)
. We therefore obtain

(4.59)

\begin{split}&\textup{{(LHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{rL-phi-commuted-prep}}\text{{)}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}L((1+r)^{\delta}{}(G+rG^{\prime})((rL)\varphi{})^{2})+\frac{1}{2}(G-rG^{\prime}-r^{2}G^{\prime\prime}-\delta{}(1+r)^{-1})r(1+r)^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+(1+r)^{\delta}{}\Bigl(r^{1/2}L(rL)\varphi{}+\frac{1}{2}r^{1/2}L\varphi{}\Bigr)(2rL\Psi{}-rG^{\prime}\Psi{})\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}L((1+O(\epsilon{}))(1+r)^{\delta}{}((rL)\varphi{})^{2})+\frac{1}{4}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}-C\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(r^{1/2}\lvert L(rL)\varphi{}\rvert+r^{1/2}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert)(r\lvert L\Psi{}\rvert+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\lvert\Psi{}\rvert).\end{split}

for
\delta{}\geq 0
sufficiently small (say
\delta{}<1/8
). We used

\lvert rG^{\prime}\rvert\leq\langle{}r\rangle^{-1}
to pass to the final line. Substitute

eqs.

4.56
and
4.57
into
eq.

4.55

and rearrange terms to obtain

(4.60)

\begin{split}&C^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+C^{-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)\varphi{})^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}L((1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))(1+r)^{\delta}{}((rL)\varphi{})^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\leq C\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+C(\delta{}+\epsilon{})\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+C\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(r^{1/2}\lvert L(rL)\varphi{}\rvert+r^{1/2}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert)(r\lvert L\Psi{}\rvert+\langle{}r\rangle^{-1}\lvert\Psi{}\rvert+r^{3/2}\lvert(rL)\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

The final term on the left-hand side produces a term at
\underline{C}(v)
with a
good sign (and no boundary term at
\{r=0\}
). After dropping this term,
absorbing the
(\delta{}+\epsilon{})
-term to the left-hand side (taking

\delta{}
sufficiently small), and applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

(4.61)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)\varphi{})^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\Psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2+\delta{}}\Psi^{2}+r^{3}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\lvert(rL)\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Apply
eq.

4.16
to
\Psi{}
(with
p=1+\delta{}
) to control the zeroth-order
term involving
\Psi{}
on the right-hand side of
eq.

4.61
and
obtain
eq.

4.52
when
N=0
.

Step 2: The case
N\geq 1
.
Suppose
N\geq 1
and
eq.

4.52
holds
with
N-1
in place of
N
. Apply
eq.

4.61
with

(rL)^{N}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
and use
lemma

4.12
to obtain

(4.62)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)^{N}(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{N}(rL)\varphi{})^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)^{N}(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2+\delta{}}(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}+r^{3}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\lvert(rL)\Box{}(rL)^{N}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[(rL)^{N}(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2+\delta{}}((rL)^{n}\Psi)^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{3}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\lvert(rL)^{N+1}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\end{split}

To complete the proof of
eq.

4.52
with
N\geq 1
, add to

eq.

4.61
a multiple of
eq.

4.52
with
\delta{}=0
and
N-1
in place of
N
and a multiple of
eq.

4.15

applied to
\Psi{}
(with
p=1+\delta{}
).
∎

We now prove an estimate for a
(rL)^{N}T
-derivative of the solution. We can
close the estimate when
N=0
because replacing
\varphi{}
with

T\varphi{}
turns dangerous zeroth-order terms in the bulk into derivative
bulk terms that we can control using
proposition

4.13
. To prove the estimate
for
N\geq 1
, we argue as in the proof of
proposition

4.15
to commute with

(rL)
.

Proposition 4.16

(
r
-weighted energy estimate for a
T
-derivative; radially symmetric case)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,
v\geq 0
,
N\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, we have

(4.63)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)+\mathcal{A}_{3+\delta{},N}[T\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{},\end{split}

where
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZ\Box{}\varphi{}
is the inhomogeneity in the equation
satisfied by
\Psi{}
(see
eq.

2.26
) and the inhomogeneous norms

\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
were defined in

section

2.7.3
.

Proof.

Apply
eq.

4.54
with
T\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
to obtain

(4.64)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(LT\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}(T\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}-\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\lvert LT\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}T\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\end{split}

After estimating
\lvert LT\psi{}\rvert\lesssim r^{1/2}\lvert LT\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-1/2}\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert
and applying (an
r
-weighted)
Young’s inequality, we obtain

(4.65)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(LT\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[T\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}(T\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{1+\delta{}}\lvert r\Box{}T\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Control the bulk term associated to
T\varphi{}
on the right-hand side of

eq.

4.65
using
proposition

4.13
to obtain

eq.

4.63
when
N=0
. The case
N\geq 1
follows as in the
proof of
proposition

4.13
.
∎

4.2.3.
An integrated estimate for the
T
-energy

We now use the energy estimates of

sections

4.2.1
and
4.2.2
to estimate the integral of the
higher-order
T
-energy, in preparation for an argument that establishes
energy decay using the pigeonhole principle (see
section

5
).

Proposition 4.17

(Integrated estimate for the higher-order
T
-energy; radially symmetric case)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,

v\geq 0
,
N\geq 1
, and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, we have

(4.66)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1,N-1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\Psi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1,N}[\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)+\mathcal{A}_{3,N}[T\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}(L(rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{},\end{split}

where
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZ\Box{}\varphi{}
is the inhomogeneity in the equation
satisfied by
\Psi{}
(see
eq.

2.26
), the inhomogeneous norms

\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
were defined in

section

2.7.3
, and
\eta_{h}
is a parameter associated to the
hyperboloidal foliation (see
section

2.3
).

Proof.

First, use
h(r)\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\eta_{h}}
, and then use
proposition

4.15
applied to

\varphi{}
and
eq.

4.30
(where
\eta_{h}
takes the
role of
\delta{}
) applied to
(rL)^{n}\varphi{}
for
0\leq n\leq N
in place of
\varphi{}

to obtain

(4.67)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-1-\eta{}_{h}}r(\underline{L}(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert T(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\lvert r\Box{}(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

We now control the final bulk term on the right-hand side of

eq.

4.67
. Now use an
r
-weighted Young’s inequality and then use

eq.

4.37
to estimate

(4.68)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert T(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\lvert r\Box{}(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}(T(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{1-\delta{}}\lvert r\Box{}(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}[(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+(\underline{L}(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{1-\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle^{-3-\delta{}}((rL)^{n}\Psi{})^{2}+r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{1-\delta{}}\lvert(rL)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

To complete the proof, substitute
eq.

4.68
into

eq.

4.67
and use
propositions

4.13
,
4.15
, and
4.16
to
control the terms on the right.
∎

5.
Energy decay and improved energy decay for time derivatives

The goal of this section is to show that energies
associated to time derivatives decay faster in time. In
section

6
,
we will show that a suitably renormalized version of
\varphi{}
(namely

\widehat{\varphi{}}=\varphi{}-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
) can be written as a time
derivative. In this way we will obtain improved decay for
\varphi{}
itself.

In
section

5.1
, we prove an abstract interpolation lemma (a
version of
[
Gaj23
, Prop. 8.2]
). We then use this lemma to prove
improved decay for time derivatives of good scalar fields in

section

5.2
and for time derivatives of radially symmetric scalar
fields in
section

5.3
.

5.1.
An abstract interpolation lemma

We formulate an abstract version of the interpolation argument used in

[
Gaj23
, Prop. 8.2]
.

Lemma 5.1

(Abstract interpolation lemma)
.

Let
\alpha{},\beta{},\delta{},D\geq 0
, and let
f:[0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{\geq 0}
be an integrable function satisfying the following assumptions:

(5.1)

\displaystyle f(0)

\displaystyle\lesssim D,

(5.2)

\displaystyle f(\tau{}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim f(\tau{})+(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}}D\quad\textnormal{for }\tau{}^{\prime}\geq\tau{}\geq 0,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau{}}^{\infty}f(\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{-\alpha{}}(1+\tau{})^{\delta}{}D+R(f(\tau{})+(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}}D)\quad\textnormal{for }1\leq R\leq\tau{}.

Then for
\tau{}\geq 0
and
\eta{}>0
, we have

(5.4)

f(\tau{})\lesssim_{\alpha{},\beta{},\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-\min(\beta{},\alpha{}+1)+\delta{}+\eta{}}D.

Remark 5.2
.

In
sections

5.2
and
5.3
, we will apply

eq.

5.4
when
f(\tau{})
is an energy defined on
\Sigma{}(\tau{})
,

D
is an initial data norm,
eq.

5.1
is the boundedness of initial
data,
eq.

5.2
is an energy estimate, and
eq.

5.3
is an
integrated energy estimate obtained by splitting the spatial integral defining
the energy
f(\tau{})
into the regions
\{r\leq R\}
and
\{r\geq R\}
.

Proof.

By
eq.

5.2
and
eq.

5.1
, we have

(5.5)

f(\tau{})\lesssim f(0)+D\lesssim D\quad\textnormal{for }\tau{}\geq 0,

and so it suffices to prove
eq.

5.4
for
\tau{}\geq 1
.

Step 1: The iteration argument.
Suppose we
have shown that

(5.6)

f(\tau{})\lesssim\tau^{-\gamma{}+\delta{}}D\quad\textnormal{for }\tau{}\geq 1

for some
0\leq\gamma{}\leq\min(\beta{},\alpha{}+1)
. We will show that
eq.

5.6
holds with

\min(1+(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1})\gamma{},\beta{})
in place of
\gamma{}

(with an implicit constant that is larger by a factor independent of

\gamma{}
). Note that the case
\gamma{}=0
of
eq.

5.6
follows
from
eq.

5.5
.

Let
\tau_{i}=2^{i}
be a dyadic sequence. Combine
eq.

5.6
with

eq.

5.3
to obtain

(5.7)

\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}f(\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim R^{-\alpha{}}\tau_{i}^{\delta}{}D+R\tau_{i}^{-\gamma{}}D\quad\textnormal{for }1\leq R\leq\tau{}_{i}.

Since
\gamma{}\leq\alpha{}+1
, we can take
R=\tau_{i}^{\gamma{}/(\alpha{}+1)}
in
eq.

5.7
to obtain

(5.8)

\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}f(\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\tau{}_{i}^{-\gamma{}\alpha{}/(\alpha{}+1)+\delta{}}D.

By the pigeonhole principle, there is
\tau_{i}^{\prime}\in[\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}]
such that

(5.9)

f(\tau{}_{i}^{\prime})\lesssim\tau{}_{i}^{-1-\gamma{}\alpha{}/(\alpha{}+1)+\delta{}}D.

By
eq.

5.2
and the dyadicity of the
\tau_{i}
, and hence of the
\tau_{i}^{\prime}
,
we can extend the estimate
eq.

5.9
to

(5.10)

f(\tau{})\lesssim\tau{}^{-\min(1+\gamma{}\alpha{}/(\alpha{}+1),\beta{})+\delta{}}D\quad\textnormal{for }\tau{}\geq 1,

as desired.

Step 2: Completing the proof.
Define a sequence
\gamma_{n}
for
n\geq 0
by

(5.11)

\gamma{}_{0}=0,\qquad\gamma{}_{n+1}=\min\Bigl(1+\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1}\Bigr)\gamma{}_{n},\beta{}\Bigr).

If
\gamma_{n}\leq\alpha{}+1
, then so is
\gamma_{n+1}
(since
\gamma_{n+1}\leq 1+(1-1/(\alpha{}+1))\gamma_{n}
). Since
\gamma_{0}=0
, it follows that
\gamma_{n}\leq\min(\beta{},\alpha{}+1)
for all
n\geq 0
. In particular, Step 1 shows that

(5.12)

f(\tau{})\lesssim_{n}\tau^{-\gamma{}_{n}+\delta{}}D\quad\textnormal{for }\tau{}\geq 1.

If
\beta{}\geq\alpha{}+1
, then by induction we have

(5.13)

\gamma{}_{n+1}=1+\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1}\Bigr)\gamma{}_{n}\implies\gamma{}_{n+1}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1}\Bigr)^{k}=\alpha{}+1-\alpha{}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1}\Bigr)^{n}.

If
\beta{}<\alpha{}+1
, then we have

(5.14)

\gamma{}_{n+1}=\min\Bigl(\alpha{}+1-\alpha{}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\alpha{}+1}\Bigr)^{n},\beta{}\Bigr),

and there is some
N\geq 0
such that
\gamma_{n}=\beta{}
for
n\geq N
. In either case, we
establish
eq.

5.4
by taking
n
sufficiently large that

\alpha{}(1-1/(\alpha{}+1))^{n}\leq\eta{}
.
∎

5.2.
Energy decay for scalar fields that satisfy an equation with a good zeroth-order term

In this section, we closely follow

[
Gaj23
, Sec. 8]
to show that energies of time derivatives of good
scalar fields decay faster in time. We first show that we can express

T
-derivatives in terms of
(rL)
-derivatives and
\partial_{\theta}{}
-derivatives.

Lemma 5.3
.

If
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
solves
eq.

4.2
with
inhomogeneity
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
, then for
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 1
, we have

(5.15)

\begin{split}&L(rL)^{N}T^{M}\Phi{}\\
&=\sum_{n=0}^{N+M}\mathcal{O}(r^{-M})L(rL)^{n}\Phi{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\sum_{n=0}^{N+M-m-1}\Bigl(\mathcal{O}(r^{-1-M+m})(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Phi{}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1-M+m})\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Phi{}+\mathcal{O}(r^{1-M+m})(rL)^{n}T^{m}F\Bigr),\end{split}

where the implied constants in the
\mathcal{O}
-notation depend on
N
,

M
, and the parameters of
eq.

4.2
.

Proof.

Use
2T=L+\underline{L}
to write

(5.16)

\begin{split}2L(rL)^{N}T\Phi{}&=LL(rL)^{N}\Phi{}+\underline{L}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}=r^{-1}L(rL)^{N+1}\Phi{}-r^{-1}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}+\underline{L}L(rL)^{N}\Phi{}.\end{split}

Rewrite the final term on the right using
lemma

4.7
to obtain
the
M=1
case of
eq.

5.15
:

(5.17)

\begin{split}L(rL)^{N}T\Phi{}&=\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})L(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}(rL)^{n}\Phi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(1)(rL)^{n}F.\end{split}

To complete the proof, induct on
M
and use
eq.

5.17
in the
induction step.
∎

Proposition 5.4

(Energy decay and improved energy decay for time derivatives)
.

Let
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
satisfy the assumptions of

eq.

4.16
. For
p\in[0,2-\eta{}_{0}]
(where
\eta_{0}
was fixed in

eq.

4.16
),
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 0
, and
v\geq 0
, define

(5.18)

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)&\coloneqq{}\sum_{m=0}^{M}\Bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N+M-m}[T^{m}\Phi{}](0,v)+\sup_{\tau{}\geq 0}(1+\tau{})^{2m}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p+1,N+M-m}[T^{m}F](\tau{},\infty,v)\Bigr),\end{split}

where
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
is the inhomogeneity in the equation
solved by
\Phi{}
(see
definition

4.1
) and the norm

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,N}[F](\tau{})
was defined in
section

2.7.3
. For
p\in[0,2-\eta{}_{0}]
,
N\geq 0
,
\tau{}\geq 0
, and
v\geq 0
, we have the energy boundedness
estimate

(5.19)

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[\Phi{}]\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}_{0}}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v),

as well as the following improved energy decay estimates for time derivatives,
where
\eta{}>0
is arbitrary:

(5.20)

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}_{0},\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+\eta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)\qquad(M\geq 1).

Proof.

First,
eq.

5.19
follows from
eq.

4.16
, so we can consider

M\geq 1
. By
eq.

4.16
, it suffices to consider
\tau{}\geq 1
.
Let
1\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and let
R\geq 1
. Split into the regions

\{r\leq R\}
and
\{r\geq R\}
and use
h(r)\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}
to obtain

(5.21)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}h(r)r^{p-2}[(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}+(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim R\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\leq R\}}r^{p-1}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+R^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p+1}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim R(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v))+R^{-1}\cdot\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M},\end{split}

where

(5.22)

\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p+1}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n_{2}}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.

To pass to the last line in
eq.

5.21
, we used
eq.

4.16
and
the estimate
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,N}[T^{M}F](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)\leq\tau{}_{1}^{-2M}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)
(which follows from the
definition of the norm
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
). To control the term

\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M}
, use
lemma

5.3
to estimate

(5.23)

\begin{split}&\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{-2(M-1)}\sum_{n=0}^{n_{2}+M}r^{p-1}(L\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}r^{-2(M-m-1)}\sum_{n=0}^{n_{2}+M-m-1}\\
&\qquad\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}\bigl(r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}+1}(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-3}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p+1}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rL)^{n}T^{m}F)^{2}\bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Using the
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate of
eq.

4.16
to estimate the
right-hand side of
eq.

5.23
, we obtain

(5.24)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M}&\lesssim R^{-2(M-1)}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}R^{2m}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N+M-m}[T^{m}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2m}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)).\end{split}

In particular, when
M=1
, we have

(5.25)

\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,1}\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,1}[\Phi{}](v).

Substitute
eq.

5.25
into
eq.

5.21
to obtain

(5.26)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim R^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,1}[\Phi{}](v)+R(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,1}[\Phi{}](v)).

Since
\tau_{2}\geq\tau{}_{1}
is arbitrary,
eq.

5.20
for
M=1
now follows
from
eq.

5.4
(where the assumptions
eqs.

5.1
and
5.2
of

eq.

5.4
follow from
eq.

4.16
and
eq.

5.26
plays
the role of
eq.

5.3
).

Now suppose that
M>1
and that we have established
eq.

5.20
for

M-1
in place of
M
. Then, returning to
eq.

5.24

and using
eq.

4.16
and the induction hypothesis (applied to
T^{m}\Phi{}

for
0\leq m\leq M-1
), we conclude that for any
\eta{}>0
and
1\leq R\leq\tau{}_{1}
, we have

(5.27)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(I)}_{R,N,M}\lesssim_{\eta}{}R^{-2(M-1)}\tau{}_{1}^{\eta}{}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v),\end{split}

where we have used the fact that (for
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 1
and
v\geq 0
)

(5.28)

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N+1,M-1}[\Phi{}](v)\leq\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v).

Substitute
eq.

5.27
into
eq.

5.21
to
get

(5.29)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim R^{-2M+1}\tau{}_{1}^{\eta}{}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)+R(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{p,N,M}[\Phi{}](v)).

for
1\leq R\leq\tau{}_{1}
. Now
eq.

5.20
follows from
eqs.

5.4
and
5.29
as in
the case
M=1
.
∎

5.3.
Energy decay for radially symmetric scalar fields

In this section, we partially extend the
results of
section

5.2
to radially symmetric scalar fields. We
first show that we can express
T
-derivatives of a radially symmetric scalar
field
\varphi{}
in terms of
(rL)
-derivatives of
\varphi{}
and of the
quantity
\Psi{}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. Then for
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 1
, we have

(5.30)

\begin{split}L(r^{1/2}(rL)^{N}T^{M}\varphi{})&=\mathcal{O}(r^{-M+1})\sum_{n=0}^{N+M}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}\\
&\qquad+\mathcal{O}(r^{-M+1})\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{m+1})\sum_{n=0}^{N+M-m-1}\bigl(\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})T^{m}\Psi{}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})L(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Psi{}\bigr).\end{split}

Proof.

The case
N=0
and
M=1
follows from the computation

(5.31)

LT\psi{}=L(r^{1/2}T\varphi{})=L(r^{1/2}L\varphi{})-L(r^{1/2}GZ\varphi{})=r^{-1/2}L(rL)\varphi{}-\frac{1}{2}r^{-1/2}GL\varphi{}-L\Psi{}.

Apply
eq.

5.31
to
(rL)^{N}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
and use

eq.

4.36
to conclude the case
M=1
:

(5.32)

\begin{split}L(r^{1/2}(rL)^{N}T\varphi{})&=r^{-1/2}L(rL)^{N+1}\varphi{}-\frac{1}{2}r^{-1/2}GL(rL)^{N}\varphi{}-L(r^{1/2}GZ(rL)^{N})\varphi{}\\
&=\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(1)L(rL)^{n}\Psi{}.\end{split}

Multiply
eq.

5.32
by
r^{1/2}
and
rearrange terms to obtain

(5.33)

L(rL)^{N}T\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})L(rL)^{n}\varphi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})L(rL)^{n}\Psi{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(r^{-3/2})(rL)^{n}\Psi{}.

The general formula
eq.

5.30
follows
by induction on
M
, where in the inductive step one uses

eqs.

5.32
and
5.33
applied to

T^{M-1}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
.
∎

Proposition 5.6

(Energy decay and improved energy decay for time derivatives; radially symmetric case)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. Suppose moreover that there
exists a function
\Phi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{r=0\})
such that

(i)

T\Phi{}=\Psi{}=r^{1/2}GZ\varphi{}
,

(ii)

and
\Phi{}
satisfies the assumptions of
definition

4.1

with inhomogeneity
\tilde{F}
.

For
N\geq 1
,
M\geq 0
,
\delta{}\geq 0
, and
v\geq 0
, define the initial data norm

(5.34)

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)&\coloneqq{}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M+1}[\Phi{}](v)+\sum_{m=0}^{M}\bigl(E_{N+M-m}[T^{m}\varphi{}](0,v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N+M-m}[T^{m}\varphi{}](0,v)\bigr)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{M}\bigl(\sup_{\tau{}\geq 0}(1+\tau{})^{2m+2}\mathcal{A}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{m}\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{},\infty,v)+\sup_{\tau{}\geq 0}(1+\tau{})^{2m}\mathcal{A}_{3+\delta{},N}[T^{m}\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{},\infty,v)\bigr),\\
\end{split}

where the data norm
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
was defined in
eq.

5.20
and
the norm
\mathcal{A}
of the inhomogeneity was defined in
section

2.7.3
.

Then for all
N\geq 1
,
\tau{}\geq 0
, and
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small
(that the estimates of
section

4.2.2
hold),
we have the energy boundedness and decay estimates

(5.35)

\displaystyle E_{N}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{N,0,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[(rL)\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,0,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v),

as well as the following improved energy decay estimates for time derivatives,
where
M\geq 1
and
\eta{}>0
:

(5.37)

\displaystyle E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{},\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1-2M+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v),

(5.38)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta,N-1}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{},\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v),

(5.39)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta,N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{},\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+1+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).

Remark 5.7

(Sharpness of the decay rates)
.

Note that, although we do not obtain the sharp decay rate (on Minkowski or given
by
theorem

3.1
) for
\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T\varphi{}]
(namely

\tau^{-2}
), we still obtain the sharp rate
\tau^{-3}
for

E_{N}[T\varphi{}]
. This is crucial for the sharp energy decay and pointwise
estimates proven in
section

7
.

Proof.

Fix
v\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, and let
\eta{}>0
.

Step 1: Preliminary estimates.
Let
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. First, we claim that

(5.40)

\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\eta}{}(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+\eta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M+1}[\Phi{}](v).

when
N\geq 1
and
M\geq 0
.
We delay the proof to the end of this step. We now note several consequences of

eq.

5.40
and the energy-estimates of
section

4.2
. We have:

(5.41)

\begin{split}&E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}(T(rL)^{n}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M-2+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\end{split}

when
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0
, and

(5.42)

\begin{split}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\eta}{}E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1,N-1}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\end{split}

when
N\geq 1
and
M\geq 0
, and

(5.43)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+1,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\end{split}

when
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0
, and

(5.44)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+E_{N}[T^{M-1}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\end{split}

when
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 1
.
Indeed,
eqs.

5.41
,
5.42
,
5.43
, and
5.44
follow immediately from

propositions

4.13
,
4.17
,
4.15
, and
4.16
combined with

eq.

5.20
once
eq.

5.40
has been established.

We now establish
eq.

5.40
. Use
lemma

5.3
(applied to
T^{M}\Phi{}
),

eq.

4.16
(applied to
T^{M}\Phi{}
with
p=1+\delta{}
), and
eq.

5.20
:

(5.45)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n}TT^{M}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta{}}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}((rL)^{n}T^{M}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}((rL)^{n}T^{M}\tilde{F})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}\Phi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2+\delta{},N}[T^{M}\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v)\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+\eta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M}[\Phi{}](v).\end{split}

Step 2: Decay for the
T
-energy from decay for the
r
-weighted energy;
proof of
eq.

5.37
from
eq.

5.38
.
The point of this step is that the
integral of
E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}]
is controlled by

\mathcal{E}_{1,N-1}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}]
(see
eq.

5.42
), and so a pigeonhole argument can
obtain a decay rate
\tau^{-1}
faster for the former than for the latter.

Let
N\geq 1
and
M\geq 0
. In this step we will show that for
M\geq 0
,
the statement

(5.46)

\mathcal{E}_{1,N-1}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\lesssim(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\textnormal{ for all }\tau{}\geq 0

for some
\beta{}\leq 2M
implies the statement

(5.47)

E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\lesssim_{\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1-\beta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\textnormal{ for all }\tau{}\geq 0.

In particular,
eq.

5.37
follows from

eq.

5.38
.

First,
eq.

5.41
implies that it suffices to
consider
\tau{}\geq 1
. Now suppose we have shown that for some
k\geq 0
and all
\tau{}\geq 1
, we have

(5.48)

E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\leq\tau{}^{-k}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).

By
eq.

5.41
, the estimate

eq.

5.48
holds for
k=0
. Let
\tau_{i}=2^{i}
be a dyadic sequence. By

eq.

5.46
and
eq.

5.48

together with
eq.

5.42
, we have

(5.49)

\int_{\tau{}_{i}}^{\tau{}_{i+1}}E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\eta}{}\tau{}_{i}^{-\min(k,\beta{})+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).

By the pigeonhole principle, there is

\tau{}_{i}^{\prime}\in[\tau{}_{i},\tau{}_{i+1}]
for which

(5.50)

E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{i}^{\prime},v)\lesssim_{\eta}{}\tau{}_{i}^{\prime-1-\min(k,\beta{})+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).

By
eq.

5.42
,

eq.

5.46
, and the dyadicity of the
\tau_{i}
(and hence of the
\tau{}_{i}^{\prime}
), we
can extend this to

(5.51)

E_{N}[T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\lesssim_{\eta}{}\tau{}^{-1-\min(k,\beta{})+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)

for all
\tau{}\geq 1
. If
k+1\leq\beta{}
, then this implies
eq.

5.48
for
k+1-\eta{}
in
place of
k
. We can repeat this argument until
k\geq\beta{}
, in which
case
eq.

5.51
implies the desired
eq.

5.47
(with
2M\eta{}
in place of

\eta{}
).

Step 3: The interpolation argument.
Fix
j\in\{0,1\}
and
N\geq 0
