such that

N+j\geq 1
, fix
M\geq 1
, and fix
1\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. Let
1\leq R\leq\tau{}_{i}
. The
goal of this step is to show that

(5.52)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}R^{-2M+1+\delta{}}\tau{}_{1}^{\eta}{}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\\
&\qquad+R(\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1-j)E_{N+j}[T^{M-1}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)).\end{split}

To produce this estimate, we will expand the integral and interpolate between
(i.e. split the region of integration into) a large-
r
region (which produces
the term on the first line) and a small-
r
region (which produces the terms
on the last line).

Split into the regions
\{r\leq R\}
and
\{r\geq R\}
and use

h(r)\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}
to obtain

(5.53)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\textnormal{(I)}_{N,j,M,R}+R^{-1+\delta{}}\textnormal{(II)}_{N,j,M,R}

for

(5.54)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(I)}_{N,j,M,R}&\coloneqq{}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq R}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(r^{1/2}(rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}((rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
\end{split}

and

(5.55)

\textnormal{(II)}_{N,j,M,R}\coloneqq{}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{2}(L(r^{1/2}(rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\

We now control the terms on the right-hand side of
eq.

5.53
. By estimating

(L(r^{1/2}f))^{2}\lesssim r(Lf)^{2}+r^{-1}f^{2}
and using the fact that the
first term in
\textnormal{(I)}_{N,j,M,R}
is only integrated over
\{r\leq R\}
(and
R\geq 1
), we obtain

(5.56)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(I)}_{N,j,M,R}\lesssim R\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-1+\delta{}}((rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

When
j=0
, we have
N\geq 1
, and so we can estimate the first term on the
left-hand side of
eq.

5.56
using
eq.

5.43
(with
N-1
in place of
N
),
and since
M\geq 1
, we can estimate the second term on the left-hand side of

eq.

5.56
by writing
(rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{}=T(rL)^{n}T^{M-1}\varphi{}

and using
eq.

5.41
(with
M-1
in place of
M
):

(5.57)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(I)}_{N,0,M,R}&\lesssim_{\eta}{}R(\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+E_{N}[T^{M-1}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)).\\
\end{split}

When
j=1
, we estimate the the first term on the left-hand side of
eq.

5.56

using
eq.

5.43
, and we estimate the second term on the left-hand side of

eq.

5.56
by writing
((rL)^{n+j}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}=r^{2}(L(rL)^{n}T^{M}\varphi{})^{2}
and using
eq.

5.43
:

(5.58)

\textnormal{(I)}_{N,1,M,R}\lesssim_{\eta}{}R(\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)).

Combining
eqs.

5.57
and
5.58
, we obtain

(5.59)

\textnormal{(I)}_{N,1,M,R}\lesssim_{\eta}{}R(\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1-j)E_{N+j}[T^{M-1}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)).

Next, we estimate
\textnormal{(II)}_{N,j,M,R}
: the calculation in
eq.

5.30
, together with the fact that the
integral is over
\{r\geq R\}
, implies that for
R\leq\tau_{1}
, we have

(5.60)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(II)}_{N,j,M,R}&\lesssim R^{-2M+2}\Bigl[\sum_{n=0}^{N+M+j}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L(rL)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}R^{2m+2}\sum_{n=0}^{N+M+j-m-1}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(L(rL)^{n}T^{m+1}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(T^{m+1}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr]\\
&\lesssim R^{-2M+2}\Bigl[\mathcal{E}_{1,N+M+j-1}[(rL)\varphi{}](0,v)+\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1,N+j,M+1}[\Phi{}](v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}R^{2m+2}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1,N+j+M-m-1}[T^{m+1}\Phi{}]+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1,N+j+M-m-1}[T^{m+1}\tilde{F}](\tau{}_{1}))\Bigr]\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}R^{-2M+2}\tau{}_{1}^{\eta}{}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,0}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).\end{split}

To pass to the second last line, we applied
proposition

4.15
(with
N+M+j-1

in place of
N
), estimating the terms that arise on the right-hand side using

eqs.

5.40
and
5.20
, and applied
eq.

4.16
(in which the norm

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
was defined). To pass to the last line, we used
eq.

5.20
.

Combining
eqs.

5.59
,
5.60
, and
5.53
, we obtain
eq.

5.52
.

Step 4: The iteration argument: proof of
eqs.

5.39
and
5.38
.
By
Steps 1 and 2, it suffices to prove the estimates

eqs.

5.39
and
5.38
for
\tau{}\geq 1
. First, note that

eq.

5.38
holds for
M=0
by
eq.

5.43
. Now suppose that

M\geq 1
and we have established
eq.

5.38
for
M-1
. By Step 2,
for
\tau{}\geq 0
and
N\geq 0
we have

(5.61)

\begin{split}E_{N+1}[T^{M-1}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)&\lesssim_{\eta}{}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+1+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+1,M-1,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)\lesssim(1+\tau{})^{-2M+1+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).\end{split}

Now let
j\in\{0,1\}
and let
N\geq 0
satisfy
N+j\geq 1
. By Step 3 and
eq.

5.61
, we
have

(5.62)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\eta}{}R^{-2M+1+\delta{}}\tau{}_{1}^{\eta}{}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)+R(\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\tau{}_{1}^{-2M+(1-j)+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v)).\end{split}

By
eqs.

5.43
and
5.44
, we have

(5.63)

\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)\lesssim_{\eta}{}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{}_{1})^{-2M+(1-j)+\delta{}+\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v).

It now follows from
eqs.

5.4
,
5.62
, and
5.63
that

(5.64)

\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[(rL)^{j}T^{M}\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\lesssim_{\eta}{}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+(1-j)+\delta{}+2\eta{}}\mathcal{D}_{N+j,M,\delta{}}[\varphi{},\Phi{}](v),

which establishes
eqs.

5.39
and
5.38
(with
2\eta{}
in place of

\eta{}
).
∎

6.
Constructing time integrals

In view of the results of
section

5
, we would like to show that the
difference of
\varphi{}
and a suitable multiple of the claimed leading-order
profile can be written as a time derivative of a solution to
eq.

2.19
,
so that said difference decays faster than the leading-order profile itself.
That is, we aim to construct a “time integral” of a suitably renormalized
solution
\widehat{\varphi{}}
(see
section

6.1.1
). Recall from
eq.

2.26
that

(6.1)

\mathcal{L}(G^{1/2}\psi{})=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}T\psi{}]+G^{-3/2}r^{1/2}\Box{}\varphi{},

where the operators
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{F}
were defined in

eqs.

2.24
and
2.25
. We can therefore hope to construct a “time integral” of
the renormalized solution
\widehat{\varphi{}}
by constructing a solution
\zeta{}
to
the equation
\mathcal{L}\zeta{}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}]|_{\Sigma{}_{0}}
on
\Sigma_{0}
and then
solving
eq.

2.19
with initial data

(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{},\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}_{0}})
.

In
section

6.1
, we introduce the Minkowskian solution

\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
and the renormalized solution
\widehat{\varphi{}}
. In

section

6.2
, we invert an elliptic operator to construct
functions which we use in
section

6.3
as initial data for wave
equations to construct time integrals.

6.1.
The Minkowskian solution and the renormalized solution

Define the Minkowskian solution

(6.2)

\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\coloneqq{}u^{-1/2}v^{-1/2}=4(t^{2}-\tilde{G}(r)^{2})^{-1/2}.

Then
\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. An explicit computation
shows that on Minkowski space (where
\tilde{G}(r)\equiv r
), we have

\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}=0
, where

\Box_{m}
is the wave operator on Minkowski space of
(2+1)

dimensions.

6.1.1.
The renormalized solution

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. We recall from

eq.

2.36
the linear functional
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]
of the (radially
symmetric part of the) initial data
(\varphi{}|_{\Sigma{}(0)},T\varphi{}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})
:

\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]\coloneqq{}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{1/2}G^{1/2}\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\psi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}+rG^{-1}T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{1/2}G^{1/2}\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\psi{}_{0}](r)|_{\Sigma{}(0)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r.

Here the linear operator
\mathcal{F}
was defined in
eq.

2.25
. When
\varphi{}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}
has sufficient decay
towards infinity, the final integral defining
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]

converges. The quantity
T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
is defined
in
eq.

6.10
, and the well-definedness of the quantity

\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]
(in particular the non-vanishing of the factor
involving
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
that is inverted) is established in

lemma

6.6
.

Definition 6.1

(The renormalized solution)
.

Given
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
, we define the renormalized quantity

(6.3)

\widehat{\varphi{}}\coloneqq{}\varphi{}-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}.

We write

(6.4)

\widehat{\psi{}}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}\widehat{\varphi{}},\qquad\widehat{\Psi{}}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZ\widehat{\varphi{}}.

Clearly, we have

(6.5)

\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}=\varphi_{0}-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}_{0}]\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}},\qquad\widehat{\varphi{}}_{\geq 1}=\varphi_{\geq 1},\qquad\Box{}\varphi{}=0\implies\Box{}\widehat{\varphi{}}=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}.

6.1.2.
Estimates for the Minkowskian solution

In this section, we construct and estimate the
second time integral
T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
of the
inhomogeneity associated to
\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
. In particular, we
show that the quantity
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]
defined in
eq.

2.36
is
well-defined (see
lemma

6.6
).

Lemma 6.2

(Estimating
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
and
\Psi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
)
.

We have

(6.6)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\rvert\lesssim_{N,M}u^{-1/2-M}v^{-1/2}

and

(6.7)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\Psi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\rvert\lesssim_{N,M}r^{3/2}u^{-3/2-M}v^{-3/2}.

Proof.

This is a straightforward calculation.
∎

Proposition 6.3

(Time integrals of inhomogneities associated to
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
)
.

There exists a radially symmetric function

T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
such
that
T^{2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}=\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
. Moreover, the following estimates hold for

N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}(\tau{}+r)^{-1}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}(1+\tau{})^{-1/2-M}(1+\tau{}+r)^{-3/2}.

Similarly, the quantity
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZT^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}

is radially symmetric, satisfies
r^{-3/2}\tilde{F}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
, and
satisfies the following estimates for
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0
:

(6.10)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\tilde{F}\rvert\lesssim_{N,M}\langle{}r\rangle^{-3/2}\tau{}^{-M}(\tau{}+r)^{-1}.

Remark 6.4
.

We write
T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\coloneqq{}TT^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
.

Proof.

Define

(6.11)

T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\coloneqq{}\frac{2}{\tilde{G}(r)^{2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{\tilde{G}(r)}{r}G(r)\Bigr)(t-(t^{2}-\tilde{G}(r)^{2})^{1/2}).

Using
eqs.

2.20
and
2.4
, we compute

(6.12)

\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}=\frac{1}{4}(1-(v-u)r^{-1}G)u^{-3/2}v^{-3/2}=\frac{1}{4}\Bigl(1-\frac{\tilde{G}(r)}{r}G(r)\Bigr)u^{-3/2}v^{-3/2}=2\Bigl(1-\frac{\tilde{G}(r)}{r}G(r)\Bigr)\frac{1}{(t^{2}-\tilde{G}(r)^{2})^{3/2}}.

A computation now verifies that
T^{2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}=TT^{-1}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}=\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
.
The smoothness and radial symmetry of
G
(and
lemma

2.3
) imply

(6.13)

\frac{1}{\tilde{G}(r)^{2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{\tilde{G}(r)}{r}G(r)\Bigr)=\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-3}).

Similar considerations show that
T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}\in r^{2}C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. One obtains
eq.

6.8
by considering
separately the regions
\{r\leq u/2\}
and
\{r\geq u/2\}
. For example,
when
N=M=0
, we use the binomial expansion in
\{r\leq u/2\}
and a
trivial estimate in
\{r\geq u/2\}
to obtain

(6.14)

t(1-(1-\tilde{G}(r)^{2}/t^{2})^{1/2})\lesssim\begin{cases}r^{2}/t\sim r^{2}/v&\textnormal{in }\{r\leq u/2\}\\
t\sim r\sim r^{2}/v&\textnormal{in }\{r\geq u/2\}\\
\end{cases}\implies\lvert T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}\rvert\lesssim r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-3}v^{-1},

and this estimate persists under differentiation by
(rL)
and
T
. Note
that
u\sim 1+\tau{}
and
v\sim 1+\tau{}+r
.

The smoothness of
r^{-3/2}\tilde{F}
and the estimates in
eq.

6.10
follow
from using
GZ=L-T
and
lemma

2.3
and the estimates for
T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
.
∎

Corollary 6.5

(Estimates for inhomogeneous norms associated to time integrals of
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
)
.

Let
F\coloneqq{}T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
. For
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 0
,
\tau{}\geq 0
,
v\geq 0
, and
\eta{}>0
, we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1,N}[T^{M}F](\tau{},\infty,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-3-2M+\eta{}},

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2-\eta{},N}[T^{M}F](\tau{},\infty,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2-2M},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{4-\eta{},N}[T^{M}F](\tau{},\infty,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M},

where the norm
\mathcal{A}
was defined in
section

2.7.3
.

Let
\tilde{F}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}GZT^{-2}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
be as in

eq.

6.10
. For
p\in[0,2-\eta_{0}]
,
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 0
,
\tau{}\geq 0
, and
v\geq 0
,

(6.18)

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{p,N}[T^{M}\tilde{F}](\tau{},\infty,v)\lesssim_{N,M,\eta{}_{0}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M},

where the norm
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
was defined in
section

2.7.3
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
eq.

6.10
and the definitions of the
norms.
∎

Lemma 6.6

(Well-definedness of the quantity
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]
)
.

Let
\eta_{h}>0
,
B_{h}>0
, and
\epsilon_{h}>0
be the constants defined in

item

(ii)
and
item

(iii)
of
section

2.3
,
respectively. If
\epsilon_{h}
is sufficiently small depending on
B_{h}
and
\eta_{h}
,
then

(6.19)

\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{1/2}G^{1/2}\mathcal{F}[\psi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}+rG^{-1}T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\neq{}0,

where the operator
\mathcal{F}
was defined in
eq.

2.25
and

T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
was defined in
eq.

6.10
. In
particular, the linear functional
\mathfrak{L}
introduced in
eq.

2.36

is well-defined.

Proof.

The value of the integral is continuous in the function
G
(near

G\equiv 1
, in the
C^{1}
topology), so it suffices to prove that the
integral has a sign when
G\equiv 1
. We will show that the integrand (when
G\equiv 1
), namely

(6.20)

\frac{1}{4}u^{-3/2}v^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\textnormal{(I)},\qquad\textnormal{(I)}\coloneqq{}h(r)^{2}r(v+u)+4uvrh^{\prime}(r)+4u^{2}(1-h(r)),

is positive when
\epsilon_{h}
is small enough compared to
B_{h}
. By the definition
of
\tau{}
(see
eq.

2.6
), we have
1\leq u\leq C
on
\Sigma{}(0)
,
where
C
depends only on
B_{h}
. Since the first term in

(I)
is non-negative, the assumption
item

(ii)
of

section

2.3
implies that

(6.21)

\textnormal{(I)}\geq 4(1-B_{h}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1})-4C\langle{}r\rangle^{-\eta{}_{h}}.

It follows that there is
R=R(B_{h},\eta{}_{h})>0
such that in
\{r\geq R\}
, we
have
\textnormal{(I)}\geq 1
. Next, since
h(0)=1
, we have

(6.22)

\lvert 1-h(r)\rvert\leq\int_{0}^{r}\lvert h^{\prime}(s)\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\leq\epsilon{}_{h}r.

It follows from
eq.

6.22
, the assumption
item

(iii)
of

section

2.3
and the estimate
v=u+r\leq C\langle{}r\rangle{}
on
\Sigma{}(0)
(for

C
depending only on
B_{h}
) that in
\{r\leq R\}
, we have

(6.23)

\textnormal{(I)}\geq r(2(1-\epsilon{}_{h}r)-C\epsilon{}_{h}\langle{}r\rangle{}-C\epsilon{}_{h})\geq r(2-C\epsilon{}_{h}\langle{}R\rangle{}).

If
\epsilon_{h}\ll R
, then we obtain
\textnormal{(I)}\geq r
in
\{r\leq R\}
.
∎

6.2.
Constructing time integral initial data by inverting an elliptic operator

In this section, we invert an elliptic operator to
construct functions which will be used in
section

6.3
as
initial data for wave equations that define time integrals.

6.2.1.
Scalar fields that solve an equation with a good zeroth-order term

For
f_{1}(r),f_{2}(r)=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}r^{2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-2})
, define the operator

(6.24)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}\coloneqq{}X^{2}\Phi{}-\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi{}+r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}\Phi{}.

For
p\in\mathbf{R}
and
N\geq 0
, let
\mathbf{H}_{p,N}
be the closure of

C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0)\setminus\{r=0\})
under the norm

(6.25)

\lVert\Phi{}\rVert_{p,N}^{2}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p}[(X\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}\Phi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}\Phi{})^{2}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

Lemma 6.7
.

For
\Phi{}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0)\setminus\{r=0\})
,
p\in\mathbf{R}
, and
N\geq 0
, we have

(6.26)

\lVert\Phi{}\rVert_{p,N}\lesssim_{p,N}\lVert r^{p/2}\Phi{}\rVert_{0,N}.

Proof.

This is a straightforward calculation.
∎

Lemma 6.8

(Commutation of the operator
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
with
(rX)
-derivatives)
.

For
N\geq 0
, there are constants
C_{N,n}\in\mathbf{R}
such that

(6.27)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}(rX)^{N}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}(rX)^{n}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2})(rX)^{n}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2})\partial_{\theta}^{2}(rX)^{n},

where the implicit constants in the
\mathcal{O}
-notation depend on
\alpha{}
,

f_{1}
, and
f_{2}
.

Proof.

Induction on
N
.
∎

Lemma 6.9

(A priori estimate for the elliptic operator
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}\in\mathbf{H}_{p,N}
solves

(6.28)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}.

Suppose moreover that one of the following assumptions holds:

(i)

\alpha{}>0
,

(ii)

or
\alpha{}>-1
,
\alpha{}\neq{}0
, and
\Phi{}=\Phi_{\geq 1}
.

Define
\tilde{\alpha{}}=\alpha{}
if
item

(i)
holds and

\tilde{\alpha{}}=\alpha{}+1
if
item

(ii)
holds. Then for
\eta_{0}>0
such that
\epsilon{}\ll\eta_{0}
,
\lvert p\rvert\leq 2\sqrt{\tilde{\alpha{}}}-\eta{}_{0}

and
N\geq 0
, we have

(6.29)

\begin{split}\lVert\Phi{}\rVert_{p,N}^{2}\lesssim_{p,N,\alpha{},\eta{}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p+2}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

Proof.

By density, it suffices to consider
\Phi{}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0)\setminus\{r=0\})
.

Step 1: The case
N=0
.
Compute

(6.30)

\begin{split}-r^{p}\Phi{}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}&=-X(r^{p}\Phi{}X\Phi{})-\partial{}_{\theta}{}((1+f_{2}(r))\Phi{}\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})+r^{p}(X\Phi{})^{2}+pr^{p-1}\Phi{}X\Phi{}\\
&\qquad+\alpha{}r^{p-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))\Phi^{2}+r^{p-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}.\end{split}

After integrating on
\Sigma{}(0)
and applying an
r
-weighted Young’s inequality
for the last term on the first line, we obtain

(6.31)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{4}\delta{}^{-1}p^{2}\Bigr)(X\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-2}(\alpha{}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))-\delta{})\Phi^{2}+r^{p-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon{}))(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\leq\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p}\lvert\Phi{}\rvert\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

If
item

(ii)
holds, then we use the Poincaré inequality on
S^{1}
:

(6.32)

\int_{S^{1}}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{}_{\geq 1})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\geq\int_{S^{1}}\Phi{}_{\geq 1}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

In either case, the expression on the left-hand side of
eq.

6.31
is
coercive for
\delta{}=\alpha{}-\eta_{0}
and
\lvert p\rvert\leq 2\sqrt{\tilde{\alpha{}}}-\eta_{0}
, and so we obtain

(6.33)

\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p}(X\Phi{})^{2}+r^{p-2}\Phi^{2}+r^{p-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim_{p,\alpha{},\eta{}_{0}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p}\lvert\Phi{}\rvert\lvert\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

Using Young’s inequality completes the proof when
N=0
.

Step 2: The case
N\geq 1
.
Now assume
N\geq 1
and

eq.

6.29
holds with
N-1
in place of
N
. Applying the
N=0
case to
(rX)^{N}\Phi{}
in place
of
\Phi{}
and using
lemma

6.8
, we obtain

(6.34)

\begin{split}\lVert(rX)^{N}\Phi{}\rVert_{p,0}\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p+2}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}(rX)^{N}\Phi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p+2}((rX)^{N}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\lVert(rX)^{n}\Phi{}\rVert_{p,0}.\end{split}

Adding a suitable multiple of the
N-1
case, we obtain

(6.35)

\sum_{n=0}^{N}\lVert(rX)^{n}\Phi{}\rVert_{p,0}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p+2}((rX)^{N}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

Repeating this argument with
\partial_{\theta}^{n}\Phi{}
in place of
\Phi{}
completes the proof (since
\partial_{\theta}{}

commutes with
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}
).
∎

Proposition 6.10

(Inversion of the elliptic operator
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
acting on good scalar fields)
.

Suppose that
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0)\setminus\{r=0\})
satisfies

(6.36)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{p+2}(\partial{}_{\theta}^{n_{1}}(rX)^{n_{2}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}<\infty

for some
N\geq 0
and
p\in\mathbf{R}
. Suppose moreover that either
\alpha{}=-1/4
and

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\geq 1}
, or
\alpha{}=3/4
and

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
is radially symmetric. Let
\tilde{\alpha{}}
and
\eta_{0}
be as
in
eq.

6.29
, and let
\lvert p\rvert\leq 2\sqrt{\tilde{\alpha{}}}-\eta{}_{0}
. Then there is a unique
\Phi{}\in\mathbf{H}_{p,N}
solving

eq.

6.28
, which is radially symmetric (resp. supported on angular modes
\geq 1
) if
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
is, and the following estimate holds:

(6.37)

\lVert\Phi{}\rVert_{p,N}^{2}\lesssim_{p,N,\alpha{}}\textup{{(LHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{F-norm}}\text{{)}}.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{F}\in r^{\beta}{}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
for
\beta{}\coloneqq{}\frac{1}{2}(1+\sqrt{1+4\alpha{}})
, then
\Phi{}\in r^{\beta}{}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
.

Remark 6.11
.

We could have formulated a version of
proposition

6.10
for
\alpha{}\notin\{-1/4,3/4\}
, but these are the only cases that we need in order to prove

theorem

3.1
.

Proof.

As in
[
Gaj23
, Sec. 9.1]
, define the twisted operator

(6.38)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}\Phi{}\coloneqq{}r^{p/2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}(r^{-p/2}\Phi{}).

We also introduce the associated bilinear operator
B_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}:\mathbf{H}_{0,0}\times\mathbf{H}_{0,0}\to\mathbf{R}
:

(6.39)

B_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}[\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}]\coloneqq{}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}X(r^{-p/2}\Phi{}_{1})X(r^{p/2}\Phi{}_{2})+\alpha{}r^{-2}(1+f_{1}(r))\Phi{}_{1}\Phi{}_{2}+r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{}_{1}\partial{}_{\theta}{}\Phi{}_{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

Note that
B^{(p)}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}[\Phi{}_{1},\Phi{}_{2}]=\langle{}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{(p)}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\Phi{}_{1},\Phi{}_{2}\rangle{}_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(0),\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{})}
.
One can check that for
\lvert p\rvert\leq 2\sqrt{\tilde{\alpha{}}}-\eta_{0}
, we have

(6.40)

\lvert B_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}[\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}]\rvert\lesssim_{p,\alpha{}}\lVert\Phi{}_{1}\rVert_{0,0}\lVert\Phi{}_{2}\rVert_{0,0},\qquad\lvert B_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}[\Phi,\Phi]\rvert\gtrsim_{p,\alpha{}}\lVert\Phi{}\rVert_{0,0}^{2}.

By
eq.

6.36
, the functional
\langle{}r^{p/2}\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\cdot\rangle{}_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(0),\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{})}
is continuous on

\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
. By the Lax–Milgram lemma, there exists a unique

\Phi{}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
solving

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}^{(p)}\Phi{}=r^{p/2}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
weakly, equivalently

\tilde{\Phi{}}=r^{p/2}\Phi{}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
solving

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}\tilde{\Phi{}}=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

and, by
eq.

6.29
, satisfying the estimate

\lVert\tilde{\Phi{}}\rVert_{0,0}^{2}\lesssim\textup{{(LHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{F-norm}}\text{{)}}
. Now suppose
N=1
. Since
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\partial{}_{\theta}{}\tilde{\Phi{}}=\partial{}_{\theta}{}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
, the uniqueness part of the
Lax–Milgram lemma ensures that
\partial_{\theta}{}\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
. By
lemma

6.8
and the fact that

\tilde{\Phi{}},\partial_{\theta}{}\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
, we
conclude that
(rX)\tilde{\Phi{}}
satisfies

r^{p/2+1}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}(rX)\tilde{\Phi{}}\in L^{2}(\Sigma{}(0),\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{})
. Then a Lax–Milgram argument shows that

(rX)\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0}
(with an estimate). This shows that

\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,1}
. In this way one can inductively show
that
\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,N}
and

\lVert\tilde{\Phi{}}\rVert_{0,N}^{2}\lesssim\textup{{(LHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{F-norm}}\text{{)}}
. By

eq.

6.26
, we conclude that
\Phi{}=r^{-p/2}\tilde{\Phi{}}\in\mathbf{H}_{p,N}
and
eq.

6.37
holds.

We now deduce a smoothness property of

\Phi{}
by considering the equation solved by
\tilde{\Phi{}}\coloneqq{}r^{-\beta{}}\Phi{}
,
namely

(6.41)

\begin{split}X^{2}\tilde{\Phi{}}+\frac{2\beta{}}{r}X\tilde{\Phi{}}-\alpha{}r^{-2}f_{1}(r)\tilde{\Phi{}}+r^{-2}(1+f_{2}(r))\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}\tilde{\Phi{}}=r^{-\beta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}

If
\alpha{}=-1/4
, then
\beta{}=1/2
, and so we have

(6.42)

(\Delta_{\mathbf{R}^{2}}+r^{-2}f_{2}(r)\partial{}_{\theta{}}^{2})\tilde{\Phi{}}+\alpha{}r^{-2}f_{1}(r)\tilde{\Phi{}}=r^{-\beta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}},

where we have written
\Delta_{\mathbf{R}^{2}}=X^{2}+r^{-1}X+r^{-2}\partial_{\theta}^{2}
. The
principal part of the operator on the left is a small and smooth perturbation of
the Laplacian in two dimensions with small and smooth potential (by the
assumptions on
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
), and the source on the right-hand side is smooth
by assumption. We conclude by standard interior elliptic regularity results that

\tilde{\Phi{}}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
. If instead
\alpha{}=3/4
and

\mathcal{F}
is radially symmetric, then
\Phi{}
is radially symmetric by uniqueness.
Moreover,
\beta{}=3/2
, and the equation becomes

(6.43)

\Delta_{\mathbf{R}^{4}}\tilde{\Phi{}}-\alpha{}r^{-2}f_{1}(r)\tilde{\Phi{}}=r^{-\beta{}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}},

where we have written
\Delta_{\mathbf{R}^{4}}=X^{2}+3r^{-1}X+\Delta_{S^{3}}
, and now interpret

\tilde{\Phi{}}
as a spherically symmetric function on
\mathbf{R}^{4}
. Again, elliptic
regularity for this equation is standard.
∎

6.2.2.
Radially symmetric scalar fields

Although the elliptic theory of
section

6.2.1
fails for radially
symmetric functions (because the critical value
-1/4
in the coefficient of
the inverse-square potential in the equation for
\psi{}_{0}
makes the operator
only degenerate elliptic rather than elliptic), for such scalar fields the
operator
\mathcal{L}
reduces to an ODE which we can solve via direct
integration.
8
8

8

In
[
GK25
]
, which
studies late-time tails for linear and nonlinear waves on the Schwarzschild
spacetime of three space dimensions, an elliptic operator similar to
\mathcal{L}
is inverted for
low angular modes via direct integration, while elliptic theory is used to
invert the operator acting on high angular modes.

Proposition 6.12

(Inverting the operator
\mathcal{L}
via direct integration; radially symmetric case)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty))
. Suppose that there are constants
B_{N,\delta{}}>0
such that the following estimate holds for each
N\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
:

(6.44)

\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{4-\delta{}}((rX)^{n}\mathcal{F})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\leq B_{N,\delta{}}^{2}.

Suppose moreover that we have the vanishing property

(6.45)

\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{1/2}G(r)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r=0.

Then the function
\zeta{}:(0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}
defined formally by

(6.46)

\zeta{}(r)\coloneqq{}-r^{1/2}G(r)^{1/2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho{}^{-1}G(\rho{})^{-1}\int_{0}^{\rho}{}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}

(i)

is well-defined and smooth, with
r^{-1/2}\zeta{}
bounded as
r\to 0
,

(ii)

determines an element of
r^{1/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}_{0})
if
\mathcal{F}

does, which is moreover radially symmetric,

(iii)

solves
\mathcal{L}\zeta{}=\mathcal{F}
,

(iv)

and satisfies the following estimate for each
N\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
:

(6.47)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}r(X(rX)^{n}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{}))^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2\delta{}}(X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{})))^{2}\\
&\qquad+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\\
&\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}B_{N,\delta{}}^{2}.\end{split}

Remark 6.13

(Purpose of the vanishing condition
eq.

6.45
)
.

Without the vanishing property
eq.

6.45
, the
\rho{}
-integral in

eq.

6.46
would not converge.

Proof.

We first establish
item

(i)
. We claim that

(6.48)

\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{r}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Big\rvert\lesssim_{\delta}{}B_{0,\delta{}}r^{3/2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-2+\delta{}/2}.

Indeed, using Cauchy–Schwarz and
\lvert G(s)\rvert\lesssim 1
, we obtain

(6.49)

\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{r}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Big\rvert\lesssim B_{0,\delta{}}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{r}s^{2}\langle{}s\rangle{}^{-4+\delta{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Bigr)^{1/2}\lesssim B_{0,\delta{}}r^{3/2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-3/2}.

This implies
eq.

6.48
as
r\to 0
. To also obtain
eq.

6.48
as
r\to\infty
,
we use the vanishing condition
eq.

6.45
to write

(6.50)

\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{r}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Big\rvert=\Big\lvert\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Big\rvert\lesssim B_{0,\delta{}}\Bigl(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{2}\langle{}s\rangle{}^{-4+\delta{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Bigr)^{1/2}\lesssim B_{0,\delta{}}\langle{}s\rangle^{-1/2+\delta{}/2}.

It follows that
\zeta{}
is well-defined. Moreover,
\zeta{}
inherits the smoothness
of
\mathcal{F}
on
(0,\infty)
by the fundamental theorem of calculus. Next, we establish
item

(ii)
. By assumption,
r^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}
determines a smooth radially symmetric function on

\Sigma_{0}
, and so
r^{1/2}\mathcal{F}\in rC^{\infty}(\Sigma{}_{0})
. In view of

item

(i)
of
lemma

2.3
, a Taylor expansion shows that

(6.51)

r^{-1}\int_{0}^{r}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\in{}rC^{\infty}(\Sigma{}_{0}),

and so
r^{-1/2}\zeta{}
determines an element of
C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}_{0})
, being the difference
of a constant and an element of
r^{2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})\subset C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0}).
Next,
item

(iii)
follows from the fundamental theorem of calculus and the
representation

(6.52)

\mathcal{L}\zeta{}=r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}X(rFX(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{})).

Finally, we turn to
item

(iv)
. Write

(6.53)

\textnormal{(I)}\coloneqq{}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho{}^{-1}G(\rho{})^{-1}\int_{0}^{\rho}{}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{},\qquad\textnormal{(II)}\coloneqq{}\int_{0}^{r}s^{1/2}G(s)^{1/2}\mathcal{F}(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s,

so that

(6.54)

\lvert\textnormal{(I)}\rvert=\lvert r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{}\rvert\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle^{-1/2+\delta{}/2},\qquad\lvert\textnormal{(II)}\rvert\lesssim_{\delta}{}B_{0,\delta{}}r^{3/2}\langle{}r\rangle^{-2+\delta{}/2},

by
eq.

6.48
. By differentiating
eq.

6.46
and using
G=\mathcal{O}(1)
and

G^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle^{-1})
, we obtain

(6.55)

\displaystyle(rX)^{N}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{N=0}\textnormal{(I)}+\mathcal{O}(1)\textnormal{(II)}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\mathcal{O}(r^{3/2})(rX)^{n}\mathcal{F},

(6.56)

\displaystyle X(rX)^{N}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\textnormal{(II)}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{O}(r^{1/2})(rX)^{n}\mathcal{F},

(6.57)

\displaystyle X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{N}(r^{-1/2}G^{-1/2}\zeta{}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})\textnormal{(I)}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})\textnormal{(II)}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\mathcal{O}(r)(rX)^{n}\mathcal{F}.

Now
item

(iv)
follows from

eqs.

6.54
,
6.55
,
6.56
,
6.57
, and
6.44
.
∎

6.3.
Constructing time integrals from initial data by solving a wave equation

In this section, we build time integrals of the
renormalized quantity
\widehat{\varphi{}}
(and of
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
)
using the functions constructed in
section

6.2
as initial data
for wave equations. In view of the results of
section

5
, it is
important that we can construct
two
time integrals of
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
;
this is possible because
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
solves an equation with a good
zeroth-order term. We first provide a formula comparing
(rL)
-derivatives
with
(rX)
-derivatives.

Lemma 6.14

(Comparing
(rL)
-derivatives with
(rX)
-derivatives)
.

For
N\geq 1
, we have

(6.58)

(rX)^{N}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N-1\end{subarray}}((1+\mathcal{O}(r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}))(rL)(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}+\mathcal{O}(r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1})T(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}),

and the same formula holds with
X
and
L
swapped.

Proof.

The case
N=1
follows from the explicit formula
rX=G^{-1}rL+rhT
and
the fact that
G^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
rh=\mathcal{O}(1)
(by

item

(ii)
of
section

2.3
). The general case follows by
induction on
N
. The case when
X
and
L
are swapped is analogous.
∎

Proposition 6.15

(Constructing the first time integral of
\widehat{\varphi{}}
)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
solves
eq.

2.19
. There exists

T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
solving

(6.59)

\Box{}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}

such that
TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=\widehat{\varphi{}}
and the following estimates hold
for each
N\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
:

(6.60)

E_{N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)+\tilde{E}[T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](0,v)+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](0,v)\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

where the initial data norm
\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]
was defined in
section

2.7.4
.

Proof.

Step 1: Construction.

Let
\zeta{}_{0}\in r^{1/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma(0))
be the solution to

(6.61)

\mathcal{L}\zeta{}_{0}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}_{0}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}-G^{-3/2}r^{1/2}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}

constructed in
eq.

6.47
. Note that the right
side of
eq.

6.61
is an element of
r^{1/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
and satisfies the
vanishing condition
eq.

6.45
by the definition of
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]
.
Let
\zeta{}_{\geq 1}\in r^{1/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
be the solution to

(6.62)

\mathcal{L}\zeta{}_{\geq 1}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}

constructed in
proposition

6.10
(noting that the operator

\mathcal{L}
is of the type considered in the proposition). Then
\zeta{}\coloneqq{}\zeta{}_{0}+\zeta{}_{\geq 1}
is a solution
\zeta{}\in r^{1/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
to

(6.63)

\mathcal{L}\zeta{}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}-G^{-3/2}r^{1/2}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}.

Let
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be the unique solution to

(6.64)

\Box{}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}},\qquad(T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)},TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})=(G^{-1/2}r^{-1/2}\zeta{},\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}),

where
T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
was defined in
eq.

6.10
.

We now show that
TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=\widehat{\varphi{}}
. Since

(6.65)

\Box{}TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=T\Box{}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]TT^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}=\Box{}\widehat{\varphi{}},

it suffices to show that
(\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)},T\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})=(TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)},TTT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})
,
by the uniqueness of solutions to
eq.

2.19
. The equality

TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}
holds by construction. To see that

TTT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=T\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}
, note that, by

eqs.

2.26
and
6.64
we have

(6.66)

\mathcal{L}(G^{1/2}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}})=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}TT^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}]-G^{-3/2}r^{1/2}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}

Restricting to
\Sigma{}(0)
, using the prescription

G^{1/2}r^{1/2}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=\zeta{}
, and
recalling
eq.

6.63
, we obtain

(6.67)

\mathcal{L}(G^{1/2}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}})|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=\mathcal{L}\zeta{}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}-G^{-3/2}r^{1/2}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}.

It follows from
eqs.

6.66
and
6.67
that

(6.68)

\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}TT^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\psi{}}]|_{\Sigma{}(0)}.

Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{F}
(see
eq.

2.25
), we obtain the desired equality

TTT^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}=\widehat{\psi{}}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}
.

Step 2: Estimates.
We want to control energies of
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}
that
involve
(rL)
-derivatives. We first use
lemma

6.14
to replace

(rL)
-derivatives by
(rX)
-derivatives and
T
-derivatives. We also use
the relation
TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}=\widehat{\varphi{}}
so that we
only estimate
T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}
for
M=0
and

T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}
for
M>0
. This is done in

eqs.

6.69
and
6.70
. We then estimate the
(rX)
-derivatives
using the results of
section

6.2
, which in turn requires bounding
the source term on the right-hand side of
eq.

6.63
using

eq.

6.10
; this is done in
eq.

6.71
.

First, by
lemma

6.14
and
TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}=\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
, we have

(6.69)

\begin{split}&E_{N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0,m\geq 1\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}((rX)^{n}T^{m-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}+r(X(rX)^{n}T^{m-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}+h(r)r(T(rX)^{n}T^{m-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0,m\geq 0\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\lVert\langle{}r\rangle^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))}^{2}+\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}E_{N-1-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

Similarly, we have

(6.70)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0,m\geq 1\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L(r^{1/2}(rL)^{n}T^{m-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{m-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{N}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)+v^{3\delta{}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0,m\geq 0\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\lVert\langle{}r\rangle^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))}^{2}\\
&\qquad+v^{3\delta{}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}(X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{},\end{split}

where we have used
v\sim\langle{}r\rangle{}
on
\Sigma{}(0)
. By
eq.

6.47
, we have

(6.71)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}r(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}+r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2\delta{}}(X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\\
&\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle^{4-\delta{}}((rX)^{n}\textup{{(RHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{Tinv-phi-prep-0}}\text{{)}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\end{split}

By estimating the right-hand side using
eq.

6.10
, combining with

eqs.

6.69
and
6.70
, and using
eq.

6.7
to replace

\widehat{\varphi{}}
with
\varphi{}
in energy and pointwise norms, we obtain

(6.72)

E_{N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)\lesssim v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}].

The estimates for the norms involving
\psi_{\geq 1}
are similar, but we use

proposition

6.10
with
p=0
and
p=1-\delta{}
(noting that

\mathcal{L}
is an operator of the form

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha{},f_{1},f_{2}}
considered there, with
\alpha{}=-1/4
) in place of
eq.

6.47
.
∎

We will construct time integrals of
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
not by solving the wave
equation that
\widehat{\varphi{}}
solves, but by solving the equation that
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
itself solves (see

eq.

2.26
).

Lemma 6.16

(Solvability theory for the equation solved by
\Psi_{0}
)
.

Suppose
\Phi{}_{0}
,
\Phi{}_{1}
, and
F
are radially symmetric elements of
r^{3/2}C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. Then there exists a
unique solution
\Phi{}\in r^{3/2}C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
to the equation

(6.73)

-\underline{L}L\Phi{}-\frac{3}{4}r^{-2}G\Bigl(G-\frac{2}{3}rG^{\prime}\Bigr)\Phi{}=F

that is radially symmetric and achieves the initial data
(\Phi{}|_{\Sigma{}_{0}},T\Phi{}|_{\Sigma{}_{0}})=(\Phi{}_{0},\Phi{}_{1})
.

Proof.

Observe that a radially symmetric scalar field
\Phi{}
solves

eq.

6.73
if and only if
\tilde{\Phi{}}\coloneqq{}r^{-3/2}\Phi{}

solves

(6.74)

-T^{2}\tilde{\Phi{}}+G^{2}Z^{2}\tilde{\Phi{}}+G^{2}\frac{3}{r}Z\tilde{\Phi{}}+GG^{\prime}Z\tilde{\Phi{}}+2Gr^{-1}G^{\prime}\tilde{f}=r^{-3/2}F.

This is the equation for a spherically symmetric solution to a wave equation
associated to an asymptotically flat metric in
(4+1)
-dimensions with smooth
potential and source, for which existence and uniqueness for solutions arising
from smooth data is standard. Namely,
\Phi{}
solves

eq.

6.73
if and only if
\tilde{\Phi{}}\coloneqq{}r^{-3/2}\Phi{}

solves

(6.75)

\Box{}_{\tilde{g}}\tilde{\Phi{}}+2A^{-2}Gr^{-1}G^{\prime}\tilde{\Phi{}}=r^{-3/2}F,

where
\tilde{g}
is the spherically symmetric metric

(6.76)

\tilde{g}=-A(r)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}t^{2}+A(r)^{2}G(r)^{-2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{2}+r^{2}g_{S^{3}}.

The reduction to
eq.

6.75
is motivated by the fact that the

r^{3}
volume form present in four space dimensions absorbs the

r^{-3/2}\Phi{}
term that would otherwise introduce a term that is too
singular at the origin to apply standard existence and uniqueness results.
∎

Proposition 6.17

(Constructing the first two time integrals of
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
)
.

There exists a radially symmetric function
T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}\in r^{3/2}C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
solving

(6.77)

\underline{L}LT^{-2}\Psi{}_{0}+\frac{3}{4}r^{-2}G\Bigl(G-\frac{2}{3}rG^{\prime}\Bigr)T^{-2}\Psi{}_{0}=\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]GZ(r^{1/2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}})

such that
T^{2}T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}=\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
and

T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}\coloneqq{}-TT^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}=r^{1/2}GZT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
, where
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}

was constructed in
proposition

6.15
. Moreover, the following estimate holds for
each
N\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
:

(6.78)

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](0,v)\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+1,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}],

where the initial data norm
\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]
was defined in
section

2.7.4
.

Proof.

Step 1: Construction.
Note that if a radially symmetric field

\Phi{}
solves

(6.79)

\underline{L}L\Phi{}+\frac{3}{4}r^{-2}G\Bigl(G-\frac{2}{3}rG^{\prime}\Bigr)\Phi{}=\tilde{F},

then we have

(6.80)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(G^{1/2}\Phi{})=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}T\Phi{}]-G^{-3/2}\tilde{F},

where

(6.81)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\Phi{}\coloneqq{}X^{2}\Phi{}-\frac{3}{4}r^{-2}\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{r}G^{-1}rG^{\prime}+\frac{2}{3}G^{-1}r^{2}G^{\prime\prime}-\frac{1}{3}G^{-2}(rG^{\prime})^{2}\Bigr)\Phi{}+r^{-2}A^{2}G^{-2}\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}\Phi{}.

In particular,
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
is of the form considered in

section

6.2.1
(with
\alpha{}=3/4
).

Let
\Phi^{(1)}
be the solution to

(6.82)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\Phi^{(1)}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}]+\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T(GZ(r^{1/2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}))

constructed by
proposition

6.10
. By the uniqueness of such solutions
in the Hilbert space
\mathbf{H}_{0,N}
considered in

proposition

6.10
, we have
\Phi^{(1)}=r^{1/2}GZT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}|_{\Sigma{}(0)}
, where

T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
was constructed in
proposition

6.15
. Moreover,

\Phi^{(1)}
is a radially symmetric element of
r^{3/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
.
Define
T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
to be the unique solution to

eq.

6.73
with initial data

(G^{-1/2}\Phi^{(1)},\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})
and inhomogeneity
F=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T(GZ(r^{1/2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}))

constructed in
lemma

6.16
(where the equation is satisfied by

eq.

2.26
applied to
\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
). Since

T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
and
r^{1/2}GZT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
solve

eq.

6.79
with the same initial data, they are equal by the
uniqueness statement of
lemma

6.16
.

Next, let
\Phi^{(2)}
be the solution to

(6.83)

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\Phi^{(2)}=\mathcal{F}[G^{1/2}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}]+\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]GZ(r^{1/2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}})

constructed by
proposition

6.10
. Then
\Phi{}
is a radially symmetric element
of
r^{3/2}C^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(0))
. We now let
T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
be
the unique solution to
eq.

6.73
with initial data

(G^{-1/2}\Phi^{(2)},T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}|_{\Sigma{}(0)})
and
inhomogeneity
F=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]GZ(r^{1/2}T^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}})

constructed in
lemma

6.16
(where the equation is satisfied by

eq.

2.26
applied to
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
). We argue as in
Step 1 of the proof of
proposition

6.15
to show that
TT^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}=T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
.

Step 2: Estimates.
Arguing as in the proof of Step 2 of
proposition

6.15
(using now
the estimates of
proposition

6.10
with
p=1-2\delta{}
in place of
those of
eq.

6.47
and the right-hand side of
eq.

6.83
in place
of that of
eq.

6.63
, as well as
eq.

6.10
to estimate the
inhomogeneity associated to
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
), we obtain

(6.84)

\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](0,v)\\
&\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{N-2}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](0,v)+v^{3\delta{}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{3-2\delta{}}(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{1-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\qquad+v^{3\delta{}}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)}\langle{}r\rangle^{1-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+v^{3\delta{}}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]^{2}\\
\end{split}

By
eq.

6.7
, we can replace
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
by
\Psi_{0}
(at the cost
of gaining
\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]^{2}
, which is already present) to see that all the
terms but the one involving
T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
are present in the data norm

\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]
. On one hand, by expanding

(6.85)

\sum_{n=0}^{N}X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}X(rX)^{n}(r^{1/2}GZT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})=\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\mathcal{O}(1)X(rX)^{n}\widehat{\psi{}}_{0},

we have

(6.86)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)\cap\{r\geq 1\}}\langle{}r\rangle^{3-2\delta{}}(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{1-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}(X(r^{1/2}(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}))^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}\langle{}r\rangle^{3-2\delta{}}(X(rX)^{n}\widehat{\psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{n=0}^{N+1}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}r^{-1}\langle{}r\rangle^{4-\delta{}}((rX)^{n}\textup{{(RHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{Tinv-phi-prep-0}}\text{{)}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{},\end{split}

We can estimate the final term on the right-hand side by
\mathbf{D}_{N+1,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]
as
in Step 2 of the proof of
proposition

6.15
. On the other hand, by Step 1, we
can use
proposition

6.10
with
p=0
to estimate

(6.87)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0,v)\cap\{r\leq 1\}}\langle{}r\rangle^{3-2\delta{}}(X(rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{1-2\delta{}}((rX)^{n}T^{-1}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}\langle{}r\rangle^{2}((rX)^{n}\textup{{(RHS of }}\textup{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{Phi-1-construction}}\text{{)}})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]^{2}+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(0)}\langle{}r\rangle^{2}(X(rX)^{n}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}+((rX)^{n}T\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}+((rX)^{n}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\mathbf{D}_{N,\delta{}}[\varphi{}].\end{split}

Combining
eqs.

6.86
and
6.87
and replacing
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
with

\Psi_{0}
using
eq.

6.7
, we obtain
eq.

6.78
.
∎

7.
Late-time asymptotics

In this section, we combine the results of

sections

5
and
6
to derive pointwise decay for the
renormalized quantity
\widehat{\varphi{}}
, and hence precise late-time
asymptotics for
\varphi{}
itself. In
section

7.1
, we derive energy
decay estimates, and in
section

7.2
, we derive pointwise decay
estimates.

7.1.
Energy decay for the renormalized solution

In this section, we prove energy decay estimates for the renormalized solution

\widehat{\varphi{}}
. To do this, we write
\widehat{\varphi{}}=TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}

using the time integral construction of
section

6
, and then apply
the improved decay results for time derivatives obtained in

section

5
. A caveat is that the time integral
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}

does not have a finite
p=1
energy, and so we are forced to work with a small
loss in
v
for the energy defined on the truncated hypersurface
\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)
.
In
lemma

7.2
, we show that this loss in
v
can be exchanged
for a loss in
\tau{}
-decay for the energy defined on the full hypersurface

\Sigma{}(\tau{})
. The main result of this section is
proposition

7.3
,
where we prove energy decay estimates for all the solution variables associated
to
\widehat{\varphi{}}
.

Lemma 7.1

(Estimate for the initial data of time integrals of the renormalized solution)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. For
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 0
,
\delta{}>0
, and
v\geq 0
, we have

(7.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M}[T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}],

(7.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M}[T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}],

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}{}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0},T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]\qquad(N\geq 1).

Proof.

We first establish
eq.

7.1
by considering

T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}
, which satisfies the assumptions of

definition

4.1
(see
lemmas

4.3
and
2.22
) with vanishing
inhomogeneity (since
\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
is radially symmetric). It
follows from the estimates in
proposition

6.15
and the definition of the norm

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
(see
eq.

5.20
) that

(7.4)

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M}[\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](v)\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N+M-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](0,v).

Next, we consider
T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
, which satisfies the assumptions of

definition

4.1
(see
lemmas

4.3
and
6.77
) with
inhomogeneity

F\coloneqq{}-r^{1/2}\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]GZT^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
.
It follows from
corollary

6.5
and the estimates in
proposition

6.17
that

(7.5)

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1+\delta{},N,M}[T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}]\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{m=0}^{M}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N+M-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](0,v).

Finally, we establish
eq.

7.3
by considering

T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
, which solves

\Box{}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}=F=-\mathfrak{L}[\varphi{}]T^{-1}\Box{}\varphi_{\textnormal{mink}}
. It follows
from
corollary

6.5
,
eq.

7.5
, and the definition of the
norm
\mathcal{D}
(given in
proposition

5.6
) that

(7.6)

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{N,M,\delta{}}[T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0},T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](v)&\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,\delta{}}{}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{m=0}^{M}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N+M-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](0,v)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}(E_{N+M-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](0,v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N+M-m}[T^{m}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}]).\end{split}

∎

Lemma 7.2

(Exchanging growth in
v
of the truncated energy for a loss in
\tau{}
-decay of the full energy)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
. Suppose that for all
\delta{}>0
small, there exists
a constant
D_{\delta}{}>0
such that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\leq D_{\delta}{}v^{\eta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}}

for some
\eta{}>0
and
\beta{}\in\mathbf{R}
(independent of
\delta{}
). Suppose moreover that

D_{\delta}{}\leq D_{\delta{}^{\prime}}
whenever
\delta{}\leq\delta{}^{\prime}
. Then for
\delta{}<\eta{}
, we have

(7.8)

\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[\varphi{}](\tau{})\lesssim D_{2\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}+\eta{}}.

The same result holds with
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\psi{}](\tau{},v)
in place of
\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)
.

Proof.

We use the interpolation argument of
[
Gaj23
, Prop. 10.6]
. First,
choose
A>0
large enough (independently of
\tau{}
) that

\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\}\subset\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{v\leq AR\}

for all
R\geq 1+\tau{}
. Now let
r_{i}
be a dyadic sequence with
r_{0}=1+\tau{}
, and split

(7.9)

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{})&=\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}\underbrace{r(1+r)^{\delta}{}(L\psi{})^{2}+h(r)(1+r)^{\delta}{}\varphi{}^{2}}_{\coloneqq{}(\ast{})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&=\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}(\ast{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\sum_{i\geq 0}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r_{i}\leq r\leq r_{i+1}\}}(\ast{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

By assumption, we can estimate

(7.10)

\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}(\ast{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\leq\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{v\leq A(1+\tau{})\}}(\ast{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}=\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{},A(1+\tau{}))\lesssim D_{\delta}{}(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}+\eta{}}.

Using the dyadicity of the
r_{i}
, we estimate

(7.11)

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r_{i}\leq r\leq r_{i+1}\}}(\ast{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}&\leq(1+r_{i})^{-\delta{}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{v\leq Ar_{i+1}\}}r(1+r)^{2\delta{}}(L\psi{})^{2}+h(r)(1+r)^{2\delta{}}\varphi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&=(1+r_{i})^{-\delta{}}\mathcal{E}_{1+2\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{},Ar_{i+1})\lesssim(1+r_{i})^{-\delta{}+\eta{}}D_{2\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-\beta{}}.\end{split}

Returning to
eq.

7.9
(and summing the geometric series
in
eq.

7.11
), and repeating this argument for

(rL)^{n}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
, we obtain the desired result. The
argument for
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\psi{}](\tau{},v)
is similar.
∎

Proposition 7.3

(Energy estimates for the renormalized solution)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
solves
eq.

2.19
. For
N\geq 0
,
M\geq 0
,
\tau{}\geq 0
,
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle\tilde{E}[T^{M}T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](\tau{})+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}[T^{M}T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1-2M+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{E}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1-2M+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.15)

\displaystyle E_{N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-1-2M+5\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{1-2M+5\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}]\qquad(M\geq 1).

Proof.

We first claim that

(7.17)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M,\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.18)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[(rL)T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+2\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]\qquad(N\geq 1),

(7.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{},v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}v^{3\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{1-2M+2\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]\qquad(M\geq 1).

Indeed, these are immediate consequences of
eq.

5.20
applied to

T^{-1}\widehat{\psi{}}_{\geq 1}
and
T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
(which
satisfy the relevant assumptions by
lemma

4.3
),

proposition

5.6
applied to
T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
(where

T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}
plays the role of
\Phi{}
in the theorem), and

lemma

7.1
. Now
eqs.

7.14
and
7.16
follow from

lemma

7.2
together with
eqs.

7.17
and
7.20
, and

eq.

7.13
follows from
eq.

7.14
and a standard pigeonhole argument
using
eqs.

4.6
and
4.26
. Similarly,
eqs.

7.19
and
7.18
and

lemma

7.2
imply

(7.21)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[T^{M}T^{-2}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}],

(7.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{},N-1}[(rL)T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}(1+\tau{})^{-2M+5\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}]\qquad(N\geq 1).

Note that
eq.

7.12
follows from
eq.

7.21
and a standard pigeonhole
argument using
eq.

4.16
(see
eq.

4.16
).

It now remains to establish
eq.

7.15
. By
proposition

4.13
,

eq.

7.21
, and
corollary

6.5
, we have

(7.23)

E_{N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{}_{2})\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}E_{N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{}_{1})+(1+\tau{})^{-2-2M+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}].

By
proposition

4.17
,
eq.

7.21
,
eq.

7.22
,

corollary

6.5
, and an argument as in the proof of
eq.

5.40
, we have

(7.24)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}E_{N}[T^{M}T^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}](\tau{}_{1},v)+(1+\tau{})^{-2M+5\delta{}}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+1,2\delta{}}[\varphi{}].

A pigeonhole argument using
eqs.

7.23
and
7.24
(as in Step 2 of the proof
of
proposition

5.6
) proves
eq.

7.15
.
∎

7.2.
Pointwise decay for the renormalized solution

In this section, we complete the proof of

theorem

3.1
by proving sharp pointwise estimates for
\varphi{}
, which
come from improved decay estimates for
\widehat{\varphi{}}=TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}
. We do not prove pointwise estimates for

\widehat{\varphi{}}
directly. As usual, the obstruction comes at the level
of the radially symmetric part
\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
. To get around this
obstruction, we first prove an estimate for
(rL)\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
by
interpolating between the estimates of
eq.

7.29
applied in
appropriate large-
r
and small-
r
regions. We then integrate this estimate
in the
L
-direction to future null infinity (where we pick up no boundary
term). This yields an estimate in the region
\{r\geq 1\}
. To extend the
estimate to
\{r\leq 1\}
, we integrate the estimate we have for

\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}\sim r^{1/2}Z\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
in the
Z
-direction
to
\{r=1\}
, using the integrability of
r^{-1/2}
near
\{r=0\}
. We
pursue this argument in
eq.

7.57
, completing the proof of

theorem

3.1
.

Proposition 7.4

(Pointwise estimates for general scalar fields)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
and let
\eta_{h}>0
and
C_{h}\geq 2
be the constants
defined in
item

(ii)
and
item

(iv)
of

section

2.3
. Recall the norm
\mathcal{A}
defined in

section

2.7.3
. Then for
R\geq 1
,
N\geq 0
, and
\tau{}\geq 0
, we have

(7.25)

\begin{split}&\lVert(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\lesssim_{N}R^{C_{h}}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}_{1}[T^{N}\varphi{}_{0}](\tau{})+\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq N\\
0\leq k\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{N-m}[T^{m+k}\varphi{}_{0}](\tau{})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,m\geq 0\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\mathcal{A}_{1,N-m}[T^{m}\Box{}\varphi{}](\tau{})\Bigr)\end{split}

and

(7.26)

\begin{split}&\lVert(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{\geq 1}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}\lesssim_{N}R^{C_{h}}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\Bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\psi{}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})+\tilde{E}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\psi{}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})+\mathcal{A}_{1,N-1}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\geq 1}]\Bigr)\end{split}

and

(7.27)

\lVert\Psi{}_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\lesssim R^{C_{h}+2}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\Psi{}_{0}](\tau{})+\tilde{E}[\Psi{}_{0}](\tau{})).

For
N\geq 0
,
\tau{}\geq 0
,
0<\delta{}\leq\eta_{h}
, and
r\geq 1
, we have

(7.28)

\lvert r^{1/2}(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{0}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{0}](\tau{})+E[(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{0}](\tau{})

and

(7.29)

\begin{split}&\lvert r^{1/2}(rL)^{N}\varphi{}_{\geq 1}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{N,\delta{}}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\Bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\psi{}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})+\tilde{E}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\psi{}_{\geq 1}](\tau{})+\mathcal{A}_{2,N-1}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\Box{}\varphi{}_{\geq 1}]\Bigr).\end{split}

Remark 7.5
.

We could of course formulate an
(rL)
-commuted version of
eq.

7.27

(or an analogue for general scalar fields satisfying
eq.

4.2
), as
well as a corresponding version of
eq.

7.29
, but we do not need
such estimates for the proof of
theorem

3.1
.

Proof.

Step 1: Proof of
eq.

7.25
.

Recall the following estimate for
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{2})
:

(7.30)

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{2})}\lVert\Delta f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{2})}.

Let
\chi{}(r)
be a cutoff function that is supported on
\{r\leq 2R\}
,
is identically
1
on
\{r\leq R\}
, and satisfies
r\lvert\chi{}^{\prime}(r)\rvert+r^{2}\lvert\chi{}^{\prime\prime}(r)\rvert\lesssim 1
. By applying
eq.

7.30
with
\chi{}\varphi{}

(considered as a function on
\Sigma{}(\tau{})
) in place of
f
and using the expression

\Delta=X^{2}+r^{-1}X+r^{-2}\partial_{\theta}^{2}
and the identity

(7.31)

\Delta(\chi{}\varphi{})=\chi{}\Delta\varphi{}+(\Delta\chi{})\varphi{}+2\chi{}^{\prime}X\varphi{}\implies\lvert\Delta(\chi{}\varphi{})\rvert\lesssim\lvert\Delta\varphi{}\rvert+R^{-2}\lvert\varphi{}\rvert+R^{-1}\lvert X\varphi{}\rvert,

we obtain

(7.32)

\begin{split}&\lVert\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\\
&\lesssim R^{-2}\lVert\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}+\lVert X\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}+\lVert\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}\lVert\Delta\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}.\end{split}

where the
L^{2}
norms are with respect to the volume form
r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}
.

Step 1a: Proof of
eq.

7.25
when
N=0
.
In this step we will show that

(7.33)

\begin{split}\lVert\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}&\lesssim R^{C_{h}}(\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[T\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{}](\tau{}))+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}r\lvert\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

First, we have

(7.34)

\begin{split}\lVert\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}+\lVert X\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}&=\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}\varphi^{2}r+r(X\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim R^{C_{h}+1}\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v_{R}(\tau{}))+E[\varphi{}](\tau{},v_{R}(\tau{})).\end{split}

We now estimate the Laplacian by the spatial part of the wave operator in

(\tau{},r)
coordinates, namely

(7.35)

\mathcal{P}\coloneqq{}X^{2}+r^{-1}(1+rG^{-1}G^{\prime})X+A^{2}G^{-2}r^{-2}\partial{}_{\theta}^{2},

which satisfies

(7.36)

G^{-2}\Box{}\varphi{}=\mathcal{P}\varphi{}-hG^{-1}(2-Gh)T^{2}\varphi{}-G^{-1}(2-2Gh)XT\varphi{}+G^{-1}((Gh)^{\prime}-r^{-1}(1-Gh))T\varphi{},

by
eq.

2.21
. In view of the assumptions on
h
and
G
and the
expression
\Delta=X^{2}+r^{-1}X+r^{-2}\partial_{\theta}^{2}
in
(r,\theta{})
coordinates, we have

(7.37)

\Delta=\mathcal{O}(1)\Box{}+\mathcal{O}(1)XT\varphi{}+\mathcal{O}(1)X\varphi{}+\mathcal{O}(1)\partial{}_{\theta}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)hT^{2}\varphi{}+\mathcal{O}(\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1})T\varphi{}.

It follows that

(7.38)

\begin{split}&\lVert\Delta\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}\lesssim R^{C_{h}-2}(E[\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[T\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[\partial{}_{\theta}{}\varphi{}](\tau{}))+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}r\lvert\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

Combining
eqs.

7.34
,
7.38
, and
7.32
we get
eq.

7.33
.

Step 1b: Applying
eq.

7.32
with
(rX)^{N}\varphi{}
for
N\geq 1
in
place of
\varphi{}
.
Let
N\geq 1
. The goal of this step is to show that

(7.39)

\begin{split}&\lVert(rX)^{N}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq R\})}^{2}\lesssim R^{C_{h}}\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq N\\
k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0\\
k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{N-m}[T^{k_{1}}\partial{}_{\theta}^{k_{2}}T^{m}\varphi{}](\tau{})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,m\geq 0\\
n+m\leq N\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}r\lvert(rL)^{n}T^{m}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

We first use
lemma

6.14
and the definition of the constant
C_{h}\geq 2
to estimate

(7.40)

\begin{split}\lVert(rX)^{N}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}[r^{2}(L(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}}h(T(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\varphi{})^{2}]r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim R^{C_{h}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}E_{N-m-1}[T^{m}\varphi{}](\tau{}).\end{split}

Next, we use
lemma

6.14
to obtain

(7.41)

\begin{split}\lVert X(rX)^{N}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}[(X(rL)^{n_{1}+1}T^{n_{2}}\varphi{})^{2}+(X(rL)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}+1}\varphi{})^{2}]r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{N}E_{N-m}[T^{m}\varphi{}](\tau{}).\end{split}

From the commutation formula
[\Delta,rX]=2\Delta
, we obtain

(7.42)

\Delta(rX)^{N}\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{n}(rX)^{n}\Delta\varphi{}

for some integers
C_{n}
. It follows from

eqs.

7.42
,
7.37
, and
6.14
that

(7.43)

\begin{split}\lVert\Delta(rX)^{N}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\})}^{2}&\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}[(X(rX)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}+(X(rX)^{n}T\varphi{})^{2}+(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rX)^{n}\varphi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2}(T(rX)^{n}T\varphi{})^{2}+\lvert(rX)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}]r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim R^{C_{h}-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq 1\\
k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}E_{N}[T^{k_{1}}\partial{}_{\theta}^{k_{2}}\varphi{}](\tau{})+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}r\lvert(rX)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
\end{split}

Substituting

eqs.

7.40
,
7.41
, and
7.43
into

eq.

7.32
, we obtain
eq.

7.39
.

Step 1c: Completing the proof.
We established
eq.

7.25
when
N=0

in Step 1a. Let
N\geq 1
.
Lemma

6.14
gives

(7.44)

\begin{split}\lvert(rL)^{N}\varphi{}\rvert&\lesssim\lvert T^{N}\varphi{}\rvert+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\geq 1,n_{2}\geq 0\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\lvert(rX)^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\varphi{}\rvert.\end{split}

Combining this estimate with
eq.

7.39
and
eq.

7.33
applied to
T^{N}\varphi{}
in place of

\varphi{}
, we obtain
eq.

7.25
when
N\geq 1
.

Step 2: Proof of
eq.

7.26
.
Let
\chi{}
be as in Step 1. First, estimate

(7.45)

\begin{split}\lvert X((\chi{}\varphi{})^{2})\rvert&\leq 2\chi^{2}\lvert\varphi{}X\varphi{}\rvert+2\lvert\chi{}\chi{}^{\prime}\rvert\varphi^{2}\lesssim r(X\varphi{})^{2}+r^{-1}\varphi^{2}\lesssim(X\psi{})^{2}+r^{-2}\psi{}^{2}\lesssim(X\psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}}h(r)r^{-2}\psi^{2}\\
&\lesssim(L\psi{})^{2}+h^{2}(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}}h(r)r^{-2}\psi^{2}.\end{split}

Since there are constants
C_{N,n}
such that

(7.46)

r^{1/2}(rL)^{N}\varphi{}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}C_{N,n}(rL)^{n}\psi{},

we obtain from
eq.

7.45
(applied to
(rL)^{N}\varphi{}
in place of

\varphi{}
) the estimate

(7.47)

X((\chi{}(rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2})\lesssim_{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N}[(L(rL)^{n}\psi{})^{2}+h(r)^{2}(\underline{L}(rL)^{n}\psi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}}h(r)r^{-2}((rL)^{n}\psi{})^{2}].

Use
eqs.

4.13
,
2.22
, and
7.45
to estimate

(7.48)

\sum_{n=0}^{N}(\underline{L}(rL)^{n}\psi)^{2}\lesssim(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}[(L(rL)^{n}\psi{})^{2}+r^{-2}((rL)^{n}\psi{})^{2}+r^{-2}(\partial{}_{\theta}{}\partial{}_{\theta}{}(rL)^{n}\psi{})+r^{3}\lvert(rL)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}].

Using the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
X
-direction together with

eqs.

7.47
and
7.48
, we find that for
r\leq R
, we
have

(7.49)

\begin{split}\int_{S^{1}}((rL)^{N}\varphi{})^{2}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}&\leq\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}\lvert X((\chi{}\varphi{})^{2})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim R^{C_{h}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0,N}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\tilde{E}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}r^{3}\lvert(rL)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\end{split}

Sobolev embedding on the circle (and
C_{h}\geq 2
) completes the proof of
eq.

7.26
.

Step 3: Proof of
eq.

7.27
.
Let
\chi{}
be as in Step 1. As in Step
2, we estimate

(7.50)

\lvert X((\chi{}\Psi{}_{0})^{2})\rvert\lesssim(L\Psi{}_{0})^{2}+h(r)(\underline{L}\Psi{}_{0})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}+2}h(r)r^{-2}\Psi{}_{0}^{2}.

Using the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
X
-direction together with

eq.

7.50
(and the
radial symmetry of
\Psi_{0}
), we obtain
eq.

7.27
.

Step 4: Proof of
eq.

7.28
.
Let
r_{0}\in[1/2,1]
. For
R\geq 1
, the
fundamental theorem of calculus in the
X
-direction gives

(7.51)

\begin{split}\int_{S^{1}}\lvert\psi{}(\tau{},R,\theta{})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}&\leq\int_{S^{1}}\lvert\psi{}(\tau{},r_{0},\theta{})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{1/2\leq r\leq R\}}\lvert X\psi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

Since
r
and
h(r)
are comparable to
1
in
\{1/2\leq r\leq 1\}
,
Cauchy–Schwarz gives

(7.52)

\Bigl(\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{1/2\leq r\leq 1\}}\lvert\psi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr)^{2}\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{1/2\leq r\leq 1\}}r\langle{}r\rangle^{C_{h}}h\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{}),

since
R\geq 1
. Use Cauchy–Schwarz, the integrability of

r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}
on
[r_{0},\infty)
for
\delta{}>0
, and
the identity
X=G^{-1}L-hT
to estimate

(7.53)

\begin{split}\Bigl(\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{1/2\leq r\leq R\}}\lvert X\psi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr)^{2}&\leq\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})\cap\{1/2\leq r\leq R\}}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(X\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}h(r)r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}\cdot h(r)(T\varphi{})^{2}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[\varphi{}](\tau{}),\end{split}

where in the last line we used
\delta{}\leq\eta_{h}
. Averaging
eq.

7.51

over
r_{0}\in[1/2,1]
and substituting

eqs.

7.52
and
7.53
, we obtain

(7.54)

\Bigl(\int_{S^{1}}\lvert\psi{}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr)^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta{}}[\varphi{}](\tau{})+E[\varphi{}](\tau{}).

The Sobolev embedding
W^{1,1}(S^{1})\hookrightarrow{}L^{\infty}(S^{1})
(which follows from the
fundamental theorem of calculus) combined with
eq.

7.54
applied
to
(rL)^{N}\varphi{}
in place of
\varphi{}
completes the proof.

Step 5: Proof of
eq.

7.29
.
Let
R\geq 1
. Arguing as in Step 3, we
obtain the estimate

(7.55)

\begin{split}\Bigl(\int_{S^{1}}\lvert\psi{}(\tau{},R,\theta{})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr)^{2}&\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}r\langle{}r\rangle^{\delta}{}(L\psi{})^{2}+h(r)(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}+h(r)r^{-2}\psi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{}}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}h(r)(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\end{split}

Combining this estimate with
eq.

7.48
, we find that for
N\geq 0
, we have

(7.56)

\Bigl(\int_{S^{1}}\lvert(rL)^{N}\psi{}(\tau{},R,\theta{})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr)^{2}\lesssim\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1+\delta{},N}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\tilde{E}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{})}r^{3}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\lvert(rL)^{n}\Box{}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

To complete the proof, commute the
(rL)^{N}
on the left inside the

r^{1/2}
-weight and use the Sobolev embedding

W^{1,1}(S^{1})\hookrightarrow{}L^{\infty}(S^{1})
(which follows from the
fundamental theorem of calculus).
∎

Proposition 7.6

(Pointwise estimates for the renormalized solution)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
solve
eq.

2.19
, and let
