\widehat{\varphi{}}
be
the corresponding renormalized solution defined in
section

6.1.1
.
Let
\gamma{}\coloneqq{}(C_{h}+1)^{-1}
, where
C_{h}\geq 2
(defined in

item

(iv)
of
section

2.3
) determines the polynomial rate
(in the radial coordinate
r
) at which the hyperboloidal foliation

\Sigma{}(\tau{})
becomes null. For
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0
and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, we have

(7.57)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert\lesssim\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\cdot(1+\tau{})^{-M-\gamma{}/4}\Bigl(\mathbf{D}_{\min(1,N)+M+4,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{k=0}^{1}\mathbf{D}_{\min(1,N)+M+2,\delta{}}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\varphi{}]\Bigr).

Proof.

Let
R\geq 1
, and let
\delta{}>0
be sufficiently small. Fix
N\geq 0
and
M\geq 0

and write

(7.58)

\mathbf{D}\coloneqq{}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+4,\delta{}}[\varphi{}]+\sum_{k=0}^{1}\mathbf{D}_{N+M+2,\delta{}}[\partial{}_{\theta}^{k}\varphi{}].

Step 1: The case
N\geq 1
.
Writing
\widehat{\varphi{}}=TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}+TT^{-1}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{\geq 1}
and applying
the estimates in
eq.

7.29
(with
\delta{}/5
in place of

\delta{}
) and
proposition

7.3
and estimating the inhomogeneous
terms that arise using
corollary

6.5
, we obtain

(7.59)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}\begin{cases}R^{C_{h}}(1+\tau{})^{-\min(3,2N+1)-2M+\delta{}}\mathbf{D}&\{r\leq R\},\\
r^{-1}(1+\tau{})^{-2-2M+\delta{}}\mathbf{D}&\{r\geq 1\}.\end{cases}

Taking
R=(1+\tau{})^{\gamma}{}
for
\gamma{}\coloneqq{}(C_{h}+1)^{-1}<1
and using the first
estimate in
\{r\leq R\}
and the second estimate in
\{r\geq R\}
, we
obtain the estimate

(7.60)

\lvert(rL)^{N}T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{N,M,\delta{}}u^{-1}v^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-\gamma{}-2M+\delta{}}\mathbf{D}\quad(N\geq 1),

which proves
eq.

7.57
when
N\geq 1
(after taking
\delta{}<\gamma{}/2
).

Step 2: The case
N=0
.
We now consider the case
N=0
. There are two
reasons we do not argue directly as in the case
N\geq 1
. First, the
right-hand sides of the estimates in
eq.

7.29
do not have
enough
\tau{}
-decay. For example,
eq.

7.25
includes

E[\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}]
when
N=0
, which decays like
\tau^{-2}
,
and so this estimate concludes
\tau^{-1}
decay for
\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}

near the origin. However, we need to prove that
\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
decays
faster than
\varphi{}_{\textnormal{mink}}
, which itself decays like

\tau^{-1}
near the origin. More seriously, the right-hand side of

eq.

7.25
when
N=0
includes

\mathcal{E}_{1}[\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}]
, which we do not even control (recall
that we only control
(rL)
-derivatives and
T
-derivatives of the scalar
field in the
\mathcal{E}_{1}
norm, but not the scalar field itself). For this
reason, we use the case
N=0
to control
\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
in the
region
\{r\geq 1\}
by integrating the estimate for

(rL)\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
in the
L
-direction to null infinity, and
integrate an estimate for
\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}\sim r^{1/2}Z\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}
in the

Z
-direction to obtain control in
\{r\leq 1\}
.

From
eq.

7.60
, we have

(7.61)

\lvert LT^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(\tau{},r,\theta{})\rvert\lesssim_{M,\delta{}}r^{-1}u^{-1/2}v^{-1/2}(1+\tau)^{-\gamma{}/2-M+\delta{}/2}\mathbf{D}|_{N=1}.

From
eq.

7.59
, we know
T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}|_{r=\infty}=0
,
and so we can integrate
eq.

7.61
in the
L
-direction to
null infinity (and use
1+\tau{}\sim u
and
v\sim u+r
) to obtain (in
(u,v)
coordinates)

(7.62)

\lvert T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(u_{0},v_{0},\theta{})\rvert\lesssim_{M,\delta{}}u_{0}^{-1/2-\gamma{}/2-M+\delta{}/2}\mathbf{D}|_{N=1}\cdot\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}r^{-1}v^{-1/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r,

where the integral is over a curve of constant
u_{0}
. Let

r_{0}\coloneqq{}r(u_{0},v_{0})\geq 1
. We split the integral into the regions

\{r_{0}\leq r\leq v_{0}/2\}
(where
v\sim u
) and
\{r\geq v_{0}/2\}
(where

v\sim r
). When the first piece exists, we have
u_{0}\sim v_{0}
, and so this
piece contributes logarithmically only in
u_{0}
. The second piece always
contributes like
v_{0}^{-1/2}
. Thus we have

(7.63)

\begin{split}\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}r^{-1}v^{-1/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{r_{0}\leq v_{0}/2}u_{0}^{-1/2}\int_{r_{0}}^{v_{0}/2}r^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r+\int_{v_{0}/2}^{\infty}r^{-3/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\lesssim v_{0}^{-1/2}\log u_{0}.\end{split}

Combining
eqs.

7.62
and
7.63
we
obtain

(7.64)

\lvert T^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}(u,v,\theta{})\rvert\lesssim_{\eta}{}u^{-1/2}v^{-1/2}\cdot u^{-\gamma{}/2-M+\delta{}/2+\eta{}}\mathbf{D}|_{N=1}\quad(r(u,v)\geq 1)

for any
\eta{}>0
, which implies
eq.

7.57
in the region

\{r\geq 1\}
.

We now consider the region
\{r\leq 1\}
. From
eq.

7.27
in

eq.

7.29
(with
R=1
) and
eq.

7.12
, we obtain

(7.65)

\lvert T^{M}\widehat{\Psi{}}_{0}\rvert\lesssim(1+\tau{})^{-5+4\delta{}}\mathbf{D}|_{N=0}\implies\lvert ZT^{M}\widehat{\varphi{}}_{0}\rvert\lesssim r^{-1/2}(1+\tau{})^{-5+4\delta{}}\quad\textnormal{in }\{r\leq 1\}.

Since
r^{-1/2}
is integrable near
\{r=0\}
, we can integrate

eq.

7.65
in the
Z
-direction to
\{r\leq 1\}
(which is
possible when
\tau{}\gg 1
) and use
eq.

7.57
for
N=0
in the region
\{r\geq 1\}
(which we have already obtained) to obtain

eq.

7.57
for
N=0
in the region
\{r\leq 1\}\cap\{\tau{}\gg 1\}
. The result in the region
\{\tau{}\lesssim 1\}
follows
from
eq.

7.59
.
∎

Appendix A
Failure of integrated local energy decay for radially symmetric scalar fields

In this section, we explain why, unlike in

(3+1)
dimensions, in two space dimensions, one cannot control a
zeroth-order bulk term by the
T
-energy. For this reason, the standard
strategy for proving Morawetz estimates in
(3+1)
dimensions, which
provides control of a zeroth-order bulk term, must fail in
(2+1)

dimensions. In particular, in
(2+1)
dimensions one cannot hope to directly
apply the method of Dafermos–Rodnianski
[
DR09
]
to prove

r^{p}
-weighted estimates, since this strategy requires as input an integrated
local energy decay estimate.

The obstruction lies at the level of the radially symmetric part of the scalar
field. The mechanism is the scale invariance of the energy norm in two space
dimensions (namely
k=1
and
p=0
in
proposition

A.1
). As a
consequence, one can only hope to control a zeroth-order term in the bulk if one
allows on the right-hand side an energy with an
r^{p}
-weight with
p\geq 1

(as we do in
section

4.2.2
).

Due to the necessary restriction
p\geq 1
, one can only hope to prove

r^{p}
-weighted estimates on their own, without invoking an integrated local
energy decay statement that controls
\varphi{}
itself by the
T
-energy.
However, as we show in
eq.

4.32
, we can establish an
integrated estimate for
derivatives of
\varphi{}
, provided that one is
willing to include an
r^{p}
-weighted energy of

\Psi{}\coloneqq{}r^{1/2}\partial_{r}\varphi{}
on the right-hand side. This is
the reason that our estimates for radially symmetric scalar fields
\varphi{}

are coupled to the estimates for
\Psi{}
, which is the main innovation in our
argument (see
section

1.2
and in particular
section

1.2.5

for further discussion).

To illustrate the argument, it is convenient to use energies defined on surfaces
of constant
t
.

Proposition A.1
.

For each
T\geq 1
, there exists a radially symmetric function
\varphi_{T}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{2+1})
solving the linear wave equation on Minkowski space
(\mathbf{R}^{2+1},m)
and
arising from compactly supported data on
\{t=0\}
such that for any
p\in[0,1)
and
k\geq 1
, the following estimate holds:

(A.1)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma{}_{t}\cap\{r\leq 1\}}\varphi{}_{T}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}t\gtrsim_{k}T^{2k-1-p}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{2}}(1+r)^{p}(\partial^{k}\varphi{}_{T})^{2}|_{\{t=0\}}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{},

where we write
\Sigma_{t_{0}}\coloneqq{}\{t=t_{0}\}
and

\partial^{k}\varphi_{T}\coloneqq{}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k-j}\varphi_{T}
. In particular, there is no uniform
estimate of the form

(A.2)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma{}_{t}\cap\{r\leq 1\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}t\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}_{0}}(\partial{}\varphi{})^{2}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}

for solutions
\varphi{}
to the wave equation on
(\mathbf{R}^{2+1},m)
.

Remark A.2
.

This proposition says that no uniform integrated local energy decay statement
can hold for radially symmetric scalar fields in two space dimensions, even if
one allows small growing weights in time and/or a loss of derivatives on the
right-hand side. As we use in
section

4.1
, this
obstruction does not occur for scalar fields with vanishing radial part, because
there is additional coercivity available from the angular term in the energy, by
way of a Poincaré inequality on
S^{1}
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition was communicated to the author by Georgios
Moschidis
[
Mos
]
. Fix
\chi{}:[0,\infty)\to[0,1]
such that
\chi{}\equiv 1
on
[0,1]
and
\chi{}\equiv 0
on

[2,\infty)
. Let
\varphi_{T}
be the solution to the wave equation arising
from initial data

(\varphi{}_{T}|_{\{t=0\}},\partial{}_{t}\varphi{}_{T}|_{\{t=0\}})=(\chi{}(r/2T),0)
. Since
\chi{}(r/2T)\equiv 1
on
\{r\leq 2T\}
, the
domain of dependence property for solutions to the wave equation implies that

\varphi_{T}\equiv 1
on
\{r\leq 1\}\cap\{0\leq t\leq T\}\subset\{r\leq 2T-\lvert t\rvert\}
, and so

(A.3)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma{}_{t}\cap\{r\leq 1\}}\varphi{}_{T}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}t\sim T.

On the other hand, we can compute (using the wave equation
(-\partial_{t}^{2}+\partial_{r}^{2}+r^{-1}\partial_{r})\varphi_{T}=0
to express

\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi_{T}|_{\{t=0\}}
in terms of

\partial_{r}^{2}\varphi_{T}|_{\{t=0\}}
and

r^{-1}\partial_{r}\varphi_{T}|_{\{t=0\}}
, noting that

\partial_{t}\varphi_{T}|_{\{t=0\}}=0
, and using
\Supp\chi{}^{\prime}\subset[1,2]
):

(A.4)

\begin{split}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{2}}(1+r)^{p}(\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k-j}\varphi_{T})^{2}|_{\{t=0\}}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}&\lesssim_{k}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{2}}(1+r)^{p}r^{-2j}(\partial_{r}^{k-j}\varphi_{T}|_{\{t=0\}})^{2}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim_{k}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\int_{2T}^{4T}(1+r)^{p}r^{-2j}(\partial{}_{r}^{k-j}(\chi{}(r/2T)))^{2}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\\
&\lesssim_{k}T^{-2k+2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\int_{1}^{2}(1+2Tx)^{p}\lvert\chi^{(k-j)}(x)\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}x\\
&\lesssim_{k}T^{-2k+2+p}.\end{split}

In passing from the second line to the third line, we have used the fact that

r\sim T
in the region of integration and that each
r
-derivative of

\chi{}(r/2T)
produces a power of
T^{-1}
.
∎

Appendix B
An exceptional cancellation on Minkowski space and control of a zeroth-order bulk term

In this section, we derive estimates that control a zeroth-order
bulk term associated to a radially symmetric scalar field on exact Minkowski
space
(\mathbf{R}^{2+1},m)
. We do not use these estimates in the proof of

theorem

3.1
, which concerns perturbations of Minkowski space; we aim here
to highlight the special structure in the estimates present on exact Minkowski
space.

As discussed in
appendix

A
, an estimate controlling a
zeroth-order bulk term on Minkowski space must include an
r^{p}
-weighted
energy on the right hand side with
p\geq 1
. The starting point is to prove a

p=1
estimate (see
eq.

B.1
). This is possible on Minkowski space due
to a crucial cancellation in the zeroth-order bulk term, which contributes with
a bad sign for all
p\in(0,2)\setminus\{1\}
. The
p=1
estimate
controls a flux term along ingoing null cones, which we then use to extend the

r^{p}
-weighted estimates to the full range
p\in[1,2)
, albeit with growing
weights in time on the right-hand side for
p>1
(see

eq.

B.17
).

On perturbations of Minkowski space, the cancellation that occurs on Minkowski
space for
p=1
is broken, and so we must treat the zeroth-order term in the
bulk as an error term on the right-hand side. For this reason, we do not prove

r^{p}
-weighted estimates for
\varphi{}
itself. However, we can prove

r^{p}
-weighted estimates for derivatives of
\varphi{}
, and this is enough
to close the argument. See
section

1.2.5
for further
discussion.

Proposition B.1

(
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate for
p=1
on Minkowski space)
.

Let
f(r)=rg(r)
for
g:[0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}
a
C^{2}
function. Then for any

v\geq 0
and
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, an identity of the following form
holds for radially symmetric functions
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
:

(B.1)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\underline{C}(v)\cap\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert rL\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Proof.

This follows immediately from the multiplier identity in
eq.

4.47

with
f(r)\equiv r
(so that
g(r)\equiv 1
). On exact Minkowski space, we
have
G(r)\equiv 1
, and so the zeroth-order term in
eq.

4.47

vanishes.
∎

We now use Hardy’s inequality to prove an estimate that controls a zeroth-order
term in the bulk by the
p=1
energy, using in particular the control of the
flux term on ingoing null cones in
eq.

B.1
. However, this estimate includes
a logarithmic loss in time.

Lemma B.2

(1D Hardy inequality)
.

Fix
a,b\in\mathbf{R}
with
a<b
, and let
f,W\in C^{1}([a,b])
. If
W
is strictly
increasing, then

(B.2)

\int_{a}^{b}W^{\prime}(x)f^{2}(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}x\leq 2[W(b)f^{2}(b)-W(a)f^{2}(a)]+4\int_{a}^{b}\frac{W(x)^{2}}{W^{\prime}(x)}f^{\prime}(x)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}x.

Remark B.3
.

If
W
is strictly decreasing, then we can apply
eq.

B.2
to
-W
.

Lemma B.4

(Estimate for a zeroth-order bulk term in a compact-
r
region on Minkowski space)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be radially symmetric. Fix
0<r_{1}\leq r_{2}
. Then for
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and
v\geq 0
, we have

(B.3)

\begin{split}\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}&\lesssim\log^{2}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}\Bigl[\mathcal{E}_{1}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert rL\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr].\end{split}

Proof.

We first introduce some notation. Fix
v_{0}\geq 0
and
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. Let

v_{\star}{}
be the minimum of
v_{0}
and the value of
v
on the circle

\Sigma(\tau{}_{2})\cap\{r=r_{2}\}
. By construction, we have

(B.4)

\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{0})\cap\{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}\subset\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}.

Write
v_{\textnormal{max}}(u_{0})
for the maximum
v
-value in

\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{u=u_{0}\}
. Write

r_{\textnormal{max}}
for the largest
r
-value in
\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}
. By the definition of
\tau{}
, we have

(B.5)

r_{\textnormal{max}}\lesssim_{R}\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}.

Finally, define the truncated outgoing null cones

(B.6)

C(u_{0})\coloneqq{}\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{u=u_{0}\}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\},

where we omit the fixed parameters
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
,
v_{\star}
, and
r_{1}
from the notation.

We now apply
eq.

B.2
in the
L
-direction with the increasing weight
W(r)=\log(2+r)
to obtain

(B.7)

\begin{split}&\int_{C(u_{0})}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\int_{C(u_{0})}\frac{\varphi^{2}}{2+r}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\int_{S^{1}}\log(2+r)\varphi^{2}|_{u=u_{0},v=v_{\textnormal{max}}(u_{0})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{C(u_{0})}(2+r)\log^{2}(2+r)(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\log^{2}(2+r_{\textnormal{max}})\Bigl[\int_{S^{1}}\varphi^{2}|_{u=u_{0},v_{\textnormal{max}}(u_{0})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{C(u_{0})}r(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr],\end{split}

where the implicit constants depend on
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
. Integrate

eq.

B.7
in
u
(noting that the curves
C(u_{0})
partition the
region
\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}
) to get

(B.8)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v_{0})\cap\{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v\star{})\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\log^{2}(2+r_{\textnormal{max}})\Bigl[\int_{\underline{C}(v_{{\star{}}})\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{2},v_{\star{}})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2},v_{\star}{})\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}r(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\Bigr]\\
&\lesssim\log^{2}(2+r_{\textnormal{max}})\Bigl[\int_{\underline{C}(v_{\star{}})\{\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau{}_{2}\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{}_{2},v_{\star{}})}h(r)\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v_{0})}r(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr].\end{split}

In the first and last inequalities we used the equivalence of

\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}
and
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}
as
spacetime volume forms and of
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}
and

h(r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}
as volume forms on
\Sigma{}(\tau_{2})
. Moreover,
in the first inequality we also used observation
eq.

B.4
. To
complete the proof, substitute
eq.

B.5
into
eq.

B.8

and use
eq.

B.1
to control the terms on the right-hand side.
∎

In view of
eq.

B.3
, in order to establish an analogue of

eq.

B.1
with control of a zeroth-order bulk term, it suffices to
construct a multiplier of the form
f(r)L
which produces (via

eq.

4.47
) a zeroth-order bulk term that is positive for sufficiently
small and sufficiently large
r
. We carry out this strategy in the following
proposition, using a similar multiplier to the one used in

[
HM23
]
(in the study of an exterior obstacle problem
in two space dimensions). To state the proposition, we introduce a “
p=1+
”
energy, which has a logarithmically stronger
r
-weight than the
p=1

energy:

(B.9)

\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{},v)\coloneqq{}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}(L\psi{})^{2}+h(r)\log\langle{}r\rangle{}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.

Proposition B.5

(Estimate for a zeroth-order bulk term on Minkowski space)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be radially symmetric. For
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and
v\geq 0
,
we have

(B.10)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma(\tau{},v)}r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}(L\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\log^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\log^{2}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}\Bigl[\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}L\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr].\end{split}

Remark B.6
.

The
r
-weight in the zeroth-order bulk term controlled on the left-hand side of

eq.

B.10
is weaker than would be expected (namely two powers
of
r
less than the
r
-weight on
(L\varphi{})^{2}
) by
\log^{3}\langle{}r\rangle{}
. As the proof shows, the loss of
\log^{3}\langle{}r\rangle{}
can be improved to a loss of
\log^{2+\delta{}}\langle{}r\rangle{}
for any
\delta{}>0
. However, our proof
strategy cannot avoid the “loss in
r
-weights.”

Proof.

Let
0<r_{0}<1
be a number to be chosen. Let
g_{1},g_{2}:(0,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{>0}

be smooth functions such that

(B.11)

g_{1}(r)=\begin{cases}(1+r)^{-1}&\textnormal{in }\{r\leq r_{0}\},\\
\sqrt{\log r}&\textnormal{in }\{r\geq 2\},\\
\end{cases}\qquad g_{2}(r)=\begin{cases}0&\textnormal{in }\{r\leq r_{0}\},\\
\log r&\textnormal{in }\{r\geq 2\},\end{cases}

with the further requirement that
g_{1}
be positive, which is possible because

g_{1}(0)
and
g_{1}(2)
are positive. We now compute the relevant quantities
to obtain a coercive estimate from
eq.

4.47
. Since

(B.12)

(-rg_{1}^{\prime\prime}(r)-g_{1}^{\prime}(r))|_{r=0}=1>0,

one can choose
r_{0}
sufficiently small so that
(-rg_{1}^{\prime\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime})(r)\geq 1/2>0
in
\{r\leq r_{0}\}
. By
eq.

B.12
and further explicit computations, we conclude that

(B.13)

\begin{cases}(-rg_{1}^{\prime\prime}-g_{1}^{\prime})(r)\sim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\log^{-3/2}\langle{}r\rangle{}&\textnormal{in }\{r\geq 2\},\\
(-rg_{2}^{\prime\prime}-g_{2}^{\prime})(r)=0&\textnormal{outside }\{r_{0}\leq r\leq 2\},\\
2rg_{i}^{\prime}(r)+g_{i}(r)\sim g_{i}(r)&\textnormal{in }\{r\geq 2\},\textnormal{ where }i=1,2.\end{cases}

We now set
f(r)=rg(r)
for
g\coloneqq{}A+g_{1}+g_{2}
, where the constant
A>0
is chosen sufficiently large that
f^{\prime}(r)\geq 1
. It follows from

eq.

B.13
that

(B.14)

\begin{cases}f(r)\sim r\log\langle{}r\rangle{},\\
rf^{\prime}(r)\sim r\log\langle{}r\rangle{},\\
2rg^{\prime}(r)+g(r)\sim\log\langle{}r\rangle{},\\
-rg^{\prime\prime}(r)-g^{\prime}(r)\gtrsim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\log^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}&\textnormal{ outside }\{r_{0}\leq r\leq 2\},\\
\lvert-rg^{\prime\prime}(r)-g^{\prime}(r)\rvert\lesssim 1&\textnormal{ in }\{r_{0}\leq r\leq 2\}.\\
\end{cases}

Since
g
extends to a smooth function on
[0,\infty)
, we can apply

eq.

4.47
with
f(r)
as above to obtain

(B.15)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}(L\varphi{})^{2}+r^{-1}\log^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},t)\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq 2\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}L\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}\Box{}_{\textnormal{m}}\rvert\varphi{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

To complete the proof, use
eq.

B.3
to control the second term
on the right-hand side of
eq.

B.15
.
∎

The control of the zeroth-order bulk term in
eq.

B.10
allows
us to use
eq.

4.30
to control a bulk term involving
\underline{L}\psi{}
.

Corollary B.7

(Estimate for a bulk term involving
\underline{L}\psi
on Minkowski space)
.

Let
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be radially symmetric. For
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and
\delta{}>0
,
we have

(B.16)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-2+\delta{}}(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\log^{2}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}\Bigl[E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert r^{-1/2}r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}L\psi{}\rvert)\lvert r\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr],\end{split}

where the energy
\mathcal{E}_{1+}
was defined in
eq.

B.9
.

Proof.

Since
(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}\lesssim r(\underline{L}\varphi{})^{2}+r^{-1}\varphi^{2}
, this follows from

eq.

4.30
and
eq.

B.10
.
∎

Given the control of the zeroth-order bulk term in
eq.

B.10
and the
bulk term involving
\underline{L}\psi{}
in
corollary

B.7
, we
can use an interpolation argument to control energies with stronger

r
-weights, at the cost of adding stronger weights in
\tau{}
on the right
side.

Proposition B.8

(Estimate for a zeroth-order bulk term on Minkowski space with stronger
r
-weights)
.

Suppose
\varphi{}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
is radially symmetric. For
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,
v\geq 0
, and
\delta{}\in(0,1)
we have

(B.17)

\begin{split}&E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}+\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\varphi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim_{\delta}{}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}^{2\delta{}}\Bigl[E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert r^{-1/2}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}L\psi{}\rvert)\lvert r\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\Bigr].\end{split}

Proof.

Step 1: The multiplier estimate.
Set
f(r)=rg(r)
for
g(r)=(1+r)^{\delta}{}
.
We explicitly compute

(B.18)

\begin{cases}2rg^{\prime}(r)+g(r)\sim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{},\\
rf^{\prime}(r)\gtrsim r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta{}},\\
\lvert-rg^{\prime\prime}(r)-g^{\prime}(r)\rvert\lesssim\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}},\\
\end{cases}

By
eq.

4.47
(whose assumptions
f
clearly satisfies), we get
(after dropping the boundary term on
\underline{C}(v)
)

(B.19)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}{(\tau{},v)}}r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}(L\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\lvert r\langle{}r\rangle{}^{\delta}{}L\psi{}\rvert\lvert r^{1/2}\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}.\end{split}

Step 2: The interpolation argument.
We now control the
final term on the right-hand side of
eq.

B.19
using
an interpolation argument. Treating
\tau{}_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
, and
v
as fixed, define

(B.20)

B\coloneqq{}E[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\mathcal{E}_{1+}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1},v)+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(\lvert T\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert r^{-1/2}r\log\langle{}r\rangle{}L\psi{}\rvert)\lvert r\Box{}_{m}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{},

where the energy
\mathcal{E}_{1+}
was defined in
eq.

B.9
. It suffices to
show

(B.21)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1+\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{\delta}{}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}^{2\delta{}}B,\end{split}

since one can add a large multiple of
eq.

B.21
to
eq.

B.19

to establish
eq.

B.17
.

We now show
eq.

B.21
. On one hand,
eq.

B.10
gives

(B.22)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-1}\log^{-2}\langle{}r\rangle{}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\log^{2}\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}B.\end{split}

On the other hand, we have

(B.23)

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}\langle{}r\rangle{}^{-\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}B.\end{split}

We delay the proof of this claim to Step 3. We obtain
eq.

B.21
by
interpolating between
eqs.

B.22
and
B.23
. By this
we mean splitting the integral into the regions
\{r\leq R\}
and
\{r\geq R\}
, using
eq.

B.22
for the first region and

eq.

B.23
for the second region, and then choosing
R\sim\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}
appropriately.

Step 3: Proof of
eq.

B.23
.
First, we claim that by
eq.

B.2
and an averaging
argument, we have

(B.24)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}(1+r)^{-\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\leq 2\}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(X\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}=:\textnormal{(I)}+\textnormal{(II)}.\\

To see this, fix
R\in[1,2]
and use
eq.

B.2
in the
X
-direction with
the decreasing weight
(1+r)^{-\delta{}}
(see
remark

B.3
) to obtain

(B.25)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq 2\}}(1+r)^{-\delta{}}\varphi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\lesssim\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}(1+r)^{-1-\delta{}}\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\\
&\lesssim\int_{S^{1}}r(1+r)^{-\delta{}}\varphi^{2}|_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}\cap\{r=R\}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}+\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)\cap\{r\geq 1\}}(1+r)^{1-\delta{}}(X\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}.\end{split}

Now average
eq.

B.25
over
R\in[1,2]
, integrate in

\tau{}
, and add a zeroth-order bulk term in the region
\{0\leq r\leq 2\}

to obtain
eq.

B.24
.

It remains to control terms
(I)
and
(II)
on
the right-hand side of
eq.

B.24
. Control term
(I)

using
eq.

B.10
. For term
(II)
, use

lemma

2.2
, the estimate
h=O(\langle{}r\rangle^{-1})
,

proposition

4.15
, and
corollary

B.7
to get

(B.26)

\begin{split}\textnormal{(II)}&\lesssim\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(L\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Sigma{}(\tau{},v)}r(1+r)^{-2}(\underline{L}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\theta{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim\langle{}\tau{}_{2}-\tau{}_{1}\rangle{}B.\end{split}

This concludes the proof of
eq.

B.23
.
∎
